📋 Official Syllabus & Exam Pattern (1BMATS101)

The examination paper consists of 10 full questions carrying 20 marks each, distributed uniformly with two questions from each of the five modules. Students must answer FIVE full questions, selecting at least one full question from each module.

  • Module 1: Partial derivatives, higher-order and mixed derivatives, Euler's theorem for homogeneous functions, Jacobians and inverse Jacobians, Taylor's and Maclaurin's series for two variables, maxima and minima of two-variable functions.
  • Module 2: Vector differential calculus: gradient, directional derivative, maximum directional derivative, divergence, curl, solenoidal and irrotational fields, scalar potential, Laplacian, scale factors in curvilinear coordinates.
  • Module 3: Matrix rank by elementary row operations, row-echelon form, Gauss and Gauss-Jordan elimination, consistency of linear systems, characteristic equations, eigenvalues, eigenvectors, Cayley-Hamilton theorem, diagonalization.
  • Module 4: Vector spaces, subspaces, linear combinations, span, linear independence, basis and dimension, coordinates, inner product spaces, norms, Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, Gram-Schmidt orthogonalization.
  • Module 5: Linear transformations, matrix representations, kernel and range, rank-nullity theorem, injective and surjective maps, composition, change of basis matrices.
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MASTER FORMULA SHEET
⚡ Master Formula Sheet
MODULE 1: Partial Differentiation & Extrema
Euler's Theorem (1st Order): x u_x + y u_y = n u
Euler's Composite: If f(u) homogeneous degree n, x u_x + y u_y = n [f(u)/f'(u)]
Euler's Theorem (2nd Order): x² u_xx + 2xy u_xy + y² u_yy = n(n - 1) u
Jacobian (2D & 3D): J = ∂(u, v)/∂(x, y); J · J' = 1
Extrema Discriminant: D = rt - s² (r = fxx, s = fxy, t = fyy)
Classification: D > 0, r > 0 ⇒ Min; D > 0, r < 0 ⇒ Max; D < 0 ⇒ Saddle
MODULE 2: Vector Calculus
Gradient: ∇φ = (∂φ/∂x) î + (∂φ/∂y) ĵ + (∂φ/∂z) k̂
Directional Derivative: D_u φ = ∇φ · (a⃗ / |a⃗|)
Maximum D.D.: |∇φ| in the direction of ∇φ
Divergence & Curl: div F⃗ = ∇ · F⃗; curl F⃗ = ∇ × F⃗
Solenoidal & Irrotational: div F⃗ = 0 (solenoidal); curl F⃗ = 0⃗ (irrotational)
Radial Formulas: ∇(rⁿ) = n rⁿ⁻² r⃗; ∇ · (rⁿ r⃗) = (n+3)rⁿ; ∇²(rⁿ) = n(n+1)rⁿ⁻²
MODULE 3: Matrices & Linear Systems
Rouché-Capelli: ρ(A) = ρ([A|B]) = n ⇒ Unique; = r < n ⇒ Infinite; ≠ ⇒ Inconsistent
Characteristic Eq (3×3): λ³ - s₁λ² + s₂λ - s₃ = 0 (s₁=Tr, s₂=minors, s₃=det)
Eigenvalue Properties: Σ λ_i = Trace(A); Π λ_i = det(A)
Cayley-Hamilton: p(A) = O ⇒ A⁻¹ = (1/s₃)[ A² - s₁A + s₂I ]
Diagonalization: P⁻¹ A P = D; for symmetric matrices, Pᵀ A P = D
MODULE 4 & 5: Vector Spaces & Transformations
Subspace Criteria: 0 ∈ W and a u + b v ∈ W for all u, v ∈ W, a, b ∈ ℝ
Cauchy-Schwarz: |⟨u, v⟩| ≤ ||u|| ||v||; Triangle: ||u + v|| ≤ ||u|| + ||v||
Gram-Schmidt: u_k = v_k - Σ [ (v_k · u_i) / ||u_i||² ] u_i; e_k = u_k / ||u_k||
Rank-Nullity Theorem: dim(Domain V) = rank(T) + nullity(T)
Injectivity Criterion: T is one-to-one ⇔ ker(T) = {0} ⇔ nullity = 0
Change of Basis: [T]_{B'} = P⁻¹ [T]_B P where P is transition matrix

VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 1
QUESTION 1: First-Order Partial Derivatives of Polynomial Function
Fundamental
A. Full Question:
If u = x³ + 3x²y + 3xy² + y³, find the first-order partial derivatives ∂u/∂x and ∂u/∂y.
B. Meaning of the Question

Find the rate of change of u with respect to x (holding y constant) and with respect to y (holding x constant).

C. Basic Concept

Partial differentiation computes derivatives with respect to one variable while all other independent variables are treated as constants.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: ∂u/∂x = d[f(x, y)]/dx with y = const; Power rule: d(xⁿ)/dx = n xⁿ⁻¹.

Explanation & Conditions: ∂u/∂x denotes partial derivative with respect to x. Pure y terms differentiate to 0.

E. Given Information

u = x³ + 3x²y + 3xy² + y³.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Compute ∂u/∂x (treat y as constant)
∂u/∂x = 3x² + 3y(2x) + 3y²(1) + 0 = 3x² + 6xy + 3y² = 3(x + y)².
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: compute ∂u/∂x (treat y as constant) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Compute ∂u/∂y (treat x as constant)
∂u/∂y = 0 + 3x²(1) + 3x(2y) + 3y² = 3x² + 6xy + 3y² = 3(x + y)².
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: compute ∂u/∂y (treat x as constant) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: ∂u/∂x = 3(x + y)², ∂u/∂y = 3(x + y)²
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • State clearly which variable is held constant.
  • Show term-by-term derivative.
  • Factorize into 3(x+y)².
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Treating y as a function of x (dy/dx).
✓ How to Avoid: This is partial derivative, so y is purely an independent constant.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Missing the product constant rule 3y·(2x).
✓ How to Avoid: Keep the constant factor outside the derivative.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 1: If u = 2x³ - 4x²y + 5xy² - y³, find ∂u/∂x and ∂u/∂y.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 1
QUESTION 2: Equality of Mixed Second-Order Partial Derivatives
Fundamental
A. Full Question:
If u = x³y² + 2x²y³, verify Clairaut's theorem: ∂²u/∂x∂y = ∂²u/∂y∂x.
B. Meaning of the Question

Verify that differentiating first with respect to x then y gives the same result as differentiating first with respect to y then x.

C. Basic Concept

For smooth functions with continuous second partial derivatives, the order of differentiation is commutative.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: ∂²u/∂y∂x = ∂/∂y(∂u/∂x), ∂²u/∂x∂y = ∂/∂x(∂u/∂y).

Explanation & Conditions: Clairaut's / Schwarz's theorem guarantees equality when second derivatives are continuous.

E. Given Information

u = x³y² + 2x²y³.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Compute ∂u/∂x
∂u/∂x = 3x²y² + 4xy³.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: compute ∂u/∂x to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Differentiate ∂u/∂x with respect to y
∂²u/∂y∂x = ∂/∂y(3x²y² + 4xy³) = 3x²(2y) + 4x(3y²) = 6x²y + 12xy².
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: differentiate ∂u/∂x with respect to y to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Compute ∂u/∂y
∂u/∂y = 2x³y + 6x²y².
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: compute ∂u/∂y to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Differentiate ∂u/∂y with respect to x
∂²u/∂x∂y = ∂/∂x(2x³y + 6x²y²) = 2(3x²)y + 6(2x)y² = 6x²y + 12xy².
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: differentiate ∂u/∂y with respect to x to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 5: Conclude equality
∂²u/∂y∂x = 6x²y + 12xy² = ∂²u/∂x∂y. Hence verified.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 5: conclude equality to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: ∂²u/∂x∂y = ∂²u/∂y∂x = 6x²y + 12xy² (Verified)
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Label LHS and RHS clearly.
  • Show both first-order steps clearly before taking second-order derivatives.
  • State 'Hence verified'.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Confusing ∂²u/∂x∂y with (∂u/∂x)(∂u/∂y).
✓ How to Avoid: It is repeated differentiation, not multiplication.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Careless power subtraction.
✓ How to Avoid: Check: d(y²)/dy = 2y, d(y³)/dy = 3y².
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 2: If u = x⁴y² - 2x³y⁴, verify that ∂²u/∂x∂y = ∂²u/∂y∂x.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 1
QUESTION 3: Homogeneous Function Degree and Euler's Theorem Verification
Fundamental
A. Full Question:
Find the degree of homogeneity of u = (x² + y²) / (x + y) and verify Euler's Theorem.
B. Meaning of the Question

Determine degree n by scaling variables by t, then verify that x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y) = n·u.

C. Basic Concept

A function is homogeneous of degree n if f(tx, ty) = tⁿ f(x, y). Euler's theorem relates its first partial derivatives directly to the function value.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y) = n·u.

Explanation & Conditions: n is the degree of homogeneity.

E. Given Information

u = (x² + y²) / (x + y).

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Determine degree n
u(tx, ty) = [(tx)² + (ty)²] / [tx + ty] = t²(x² + y²) / [t(x + y)] = t¹ u(x, y). Thus n = 1.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: determine degree n to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Find ∂u/∂x using quotient rule
∂u/∂x = [(x+y)(2x) - (x²+y²)(1)] / (x+y)² = (2x² + 2xy - x² - y²) / (x+y)² = (x² + 2xy - y²) / (x+y)².
x(∂u/∂x) = (x³ + 2x²y - xy²) / (x+y)².
💡 Why this step is performed: Apply the quotient rule [v u' - u v'] / v² treating the other variable as a constant.
Step 3: Find ∂u/∂y by symmetry
∂u/∂y = (y² + 2xy - x²) / (x+y)².
y(∂u/∂y) = (y³ + 2xy² - x²y) / (x+y)².
💡 Why this step is performed: Exploit the algebraic symmetry between x and y to write the corresponding partial derivative directly.
Step 4: Add x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y)
LHS = [x³ + y³ + x²y + xy²] / (x+y)² = [(x²+y²)(x+y)] / (x+y)² = (x²+y²)/(x+y) = 1·u = RHS.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: add x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: Degree n = 1; Euler's Theorem verified: x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y) = u
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Show u(tx, ty) = tⁿ u(x, y) first.
  • State quotient rule explicitly.
  • Conclude with LHS = RHS.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Thinking degree is 2.
✓ How to Avoid: Numerator degree minus denominator degree gives 2 - 1 = 1.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Sign error in quotient rule numerator.
✓ How to Avoid: Formula is (v u' - u v')/v² with minus sign.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 3: Find degree and verify Euler's theorem for u = (x³ + y³) / (x - y).
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 1
QUESTION 4: Jacobian of Polar Coordinate Transformation
Fundamental
A. Full Question:
If x = r cos θ and y = r sin θ, evaluate the Jacobian J = ∂(x, y)/∂(r, θ).
B. Meaning of the Question

Evaluate the 2×2 determinant of first partial derivatives of x and y with respect to r and θ.

C. Basic Concept

The Jacobian represents the local scaling factor of area under coordinate transformation: dx dy = |J| dr dθ.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: J = ∂(x, y)/∂(r, θ) = (∂x/∂r)(∂y/∂θ) - (∂x/∂θ)(∂y/∂r).

Explanation & Conditions: Determinant of the Jacobian matrix formed by row 1 = [∂x/∂r, ∂x/∂θ] and row 2 = [∂y/∂r, ∂y/∂θ].

E. Given Information

x = r cos θ, y = r sin θ.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Compute derivatives of x
∂x/∂r = cos θ, ∂x/∂θ = -r sin θ.
💡 Why this step is performed: Differentiate x partially with respect to each independent variable while holding other variables fixed.
Step 2: Compute derivatives of y
∂y/∂r = sin θ, ∂y/∂θ = r cos θ.
💡 Why this step is performed: Differentiate y partially with respect to each independent variable while holding other variables fixed.
Step 3: Set up and evaluate determinant
J = (cos θ)(r cos θ) - (-r sin θ)(sin θ) = r cos² θ + r sin² θ = r(cos² θ + sin² θ) = r(1) = r.
💡 Why this step is performed: Evaluate the Hessian discriminant D = rt - s²; D > 0 and r > 0 indicates a local minimum, while D > 0 and r < 0 indicates a local maximum.
✓ G. Final Answer: J = ∂(x, y)/∂(r, θ) = r
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Write out the 2×2 determinant.
  • Substitute all 4 derivatives.
  • Use cos² θ + sin² θ = 1.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Derivative of cos θ written as +sin θ.
✓ How to Avoid: Derivative of cos θ is -sin θ.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Swapping rows and columns.
✓ How to Avoid: Keep x in row 1, y in row 2.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 4: If x = u(1 - v) and y = uv, find J = ∂(x, y)/∂(u, v).
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 1
QUESTION 5: Stationary Point and Nature of a Quadratic Surface
Fundamental
A. Full Question:
Find the stationary points of f(x, y) = x² + y² - 4x - 6y + 13 and classify them.
B. Meaning of the Question

Find points where fx = 0 and fy = 0, and use D = rt - s² to classify the point as maximum, minimum, or saddle point.

C. Basic Concept

Critical points occur where gradient is zero. The sign of the Hessian determinant D determines curvature.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: fx = 0, fy = 0; D = rt - s² where r = f_xx, s = f_xy, t = f_yy. If D > 0, r > 0 → Minimum.

Explanation & Conditions: r is concavity in x, D is overall Gaussian curvature sign.

E. Given Information

f(x, y) = x² + y² - 4x - 6y + 13.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Find first derivatives and set to zero
fx = 2x - 4 = 0 ⇒ x = 2.
fy = 2y - 6 = 0 ⇒ y = 3.
Stationary point is (2, 3).
💡 Why this step is performed: Set the first partial derivatives fx = 0 and fy = 0 to solve for all stationary (critical) points.
Step 2: Find second partial derivatives
r = f_xx = 2, s = f_xy = 0, t = f_yy = 2.
💡 Why this step is performed: Calculate the second-order partial derivatives r = fxx, s = fxy, and t = fyy to prepare for the curvature test.
Step 3: Evaluate D = rt - s²
D = (2)(2) - (0)² = 4 > 0 and r = 2 > 0.
Therefore, (2, 3) is a Local Minimum.
Minimum value f(2, 3) = 2² + 3² - 4(2) - 6(3) + 13 = 4 + 9 - 8 - 18 + 13 = 0.
💡 Why this step is performed: Evaluate the Hessian discriminant D = rt - s²; D > 0 and r > 0 indicates a local minimum, while D > 0 and r < 0 indicates a local maximum.
✓ G. Final Answer: Stationary point (2, 3) is a Local Minimum with minimum value f_min = 0.
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Solve fx = 0, fy = 0 clearly.
  • Compute r, s, t.
  • State D = rt - s² > 0 and r > 0 clearly.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Thinking r > 0 means maximum.
✓ How to Avoid: In calculus, r > 0 means concave up, which is a minimum!
⚠️ Common Mistake: Calculating s as 2.
✓ How to Avoid: ∂(2x-4)/∂y = 0 because there is no y in fx.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 5: Find the stationary points of f(x, y) = 2x² + y² - 4x + 4y + 10 and determine its nature.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 1
QUESTION 6: Chain Rule for Functions of Two Independent Variables
Intermediate
A. Full Question:
If z = e^(xy²), where x = t³ and y = t², find dz/dt using the chain rule and verify by direct substitution.
B. Meaning of the Question

Compute the total derivative dz/dt using the multivariable chain rule dz/dt = (∂z/∂x)(dx/dt) + (∂z/∂y)(dy/dt) and compare with direct single-variable differentiation.

C. Basic Concept

When z depends on x and y, and both x and y depend on a single variable t, the rate of change of z with respect to t flows through both intermediate variables x and y.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: dz/dt = (∂z/∂x)(dx/dt) + (∂z/∂y)(dy/dt).

Explanation & Conditions: ∂z/∂x and ∂z/∂y are partial derivatives of outer function; dx/dt and dy/dt are ordinary derivatives of inner functions.

E. Given Information

z = e^(xy²), x = t³, y = t².

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Compute partial derivatives of z
∂z/∂x = y² e^(xy²)
∂z/∂y = 2xy e^(xy²).
💡 Why this step is performed: Differentiate z partially with respect to each independent variable while holding other variables fixed.
Step 2: Compute ordinary derivatives of x and y
dx/dt = d(t³)/dt = 3t²
dy/dt = d(t²)/dt = 2t.
💡 Why this step is performed: Differentiate x partially with respect to each independent variable while holding other variables fixed.
Step 3: Apply the chain rule formula
dz/dt = [y² e^(xy²)](3t²) + [2xy e^(xy²)](2t)
= e^(xy²) [3t² y² + 4t xy].
💡 Why this step is performed: Apply the multivariable chain rule by summing the product of partial and ordinary derivatives.
Step 4: Substitute x = t³ and y = t² in terms of t
xy² = (t³)(t²)² = t³ · t⁴ = t⁷.
3t² y² = 3t²(t⁴) = 3t⁶.
4t xy = 4t(t³)(t²) = 4t⁶.
dz/dt = e^(t⁷) [3t⁶ + 4t⁶] = 7t⁶ e^(t⁷).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: substitute x = t³ and y = t² in terms of t to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 5: Verify by direct substitution
z = e^(xy²) = e^((t³)(t²)²) = e^(t⁷).
dz/dt = d(e^(t⁷))/dt = e^(t⁷) · d(t⁷)/dt = 7t⁶ e^(t⁷). Verified!
💡 Why this step is performed: Substitute the parametric functions directly into the outer function and differentiate to confirm the chain rule result.
✓ G. Final Answer: dz/dt = 7t⁶ e^(t⁷) (Chain rule verified by direct substitution)
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • State multivariable chain rule formula first.
  • Compute all four components ∂z/∂x, ∂z/∂y, dx/dt, dy/dt.
  • Substitute t and combine terms.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Missing the exponent power (t²)² = t⁴.
✓ How to Avoid: Remember (t²)² = t⁴, so xy² = t³·t⁴ = t⁷.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Forgetting inner derivative in dy/dt.
✓ How to Avoid: d(t²)/dt = 2t.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 6: If z = x² + y², where x = cos t, y = sin t, find dz/dt using the chain rule and verify.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 1
QUESTION 7: Euler's Theorem for Inverse Trigonometric Function
Intermediate
A. Full Question:
If u = sin⁻¹[(x + y) / (√x + √y)], prove that x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y) = (1/2) tan u.
B. Meaning of the Question

Here u itself is not a homogeneous function, but sin u is homogeneous. We apply Euler's theorem to f = sin u and relate it back to u.

C. Basic Concept

When u = g⁻¹[f(x, y)], where f(x, y) is homogeneous of degree n, we write g(u) = f(x, y). Then by Euler's theorem on g(u): x(∂/∂x)[g(u)] + y(∂/∂y)[g(u)] = n g(u) ⇒ g'(u)[x u_x + y u_y] = n g(u) ⇒ x u_x + y u_y = n g(u)/g'(u).

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: If f(u) is homogeneous of degree n, then x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y) = n [f(u) / f'(u)].

Explanation & Conditions: Here f(u) = sin u, so f'(u) = cos u, and f(u)/f'(u) = sin u / cos u = tan u.

E. Given Information

u = sin⁻¹[(x + y) / (√x + √y)].

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Rewrite u so the RHS is homogeneous
Let z = sin u = (x + y) / (√x + √y).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: rewrite u so the rhs is homogeneous to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Find the degree of homogeneity of z
z(tx, ty) = [tx + ty] / [√(tx) + √(ty)] = t(x + y) / [t^(1/2)(√x + √y)] = t^(1 - 1/2) · z = t^(1/2) z.
Thus, z is homogeneous of degree n = 1/2.
💡 Why this step is performed: Substitute tx and ty for x and y to factor out t^n and establish the degree of homogeneity n.
Step 3: Apply Euler's Theorem to z
x(∂z/∂x) + y(∂z/∂y) = n · z = (1/2) z = (1/2) sin u.
💡 Why this step is performed: Combine the partial derivatives into the linear combination x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y) and verify it equals n·u.
Step 4: Express derivatives of z in terms of u
Since z = sin u:
∂z/∂x = cos u · (∂u/∂x)
∂z/∂y = cos u · (∂u/∂y).
💡 Why this step is performed: Differentiate z partially with respect to each independent variable while holding other variables fixed.
Step 5: Substitute and solve for LHS
x [cos u (∂u/∂x)] + y [cos u (∂u/∂y)] = (1/2) sin u
cos u [x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y)] = (1/2) sin u
x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y) = (1/2) (sin u / cos u) = (1/2) tan u. Hence proved.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 5: substitute and solve for lhs to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y) = (1/2) tan u (Hence proved)
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Define z = sin u first.
  • Show z(tx, ty) = t^(1/2) z to establish degree n = 1/2 clearly.
  • Show factorizing cos u and dividing.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Applying Euler directly to sin⁻¹.
✓ How to Avoid: sin⁻¹ is NOT homogeneous! You must apply Euler to sin u.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Wrong degree calculation.
✓ How to Avoid: √t = t^(1/2), so degree is 1 - 1/2 = 1/2, not 1/2 - 1 = -1/2.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 7: If u = cos⁻¹[(x + y) / (√x + √y)], prove that x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y) = -(1/2) cot u.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 1
QUESTION 8: Jacobian Property of Inverses J · J' = 1
Intermediate
A. Full Question:
If x = r cos θ and y = r sin θ, find J = ∂(x, y)/∂(r, θ) and J' = ∂(r, θ)/∂(x, y), and verify that J · J' = 1.
B. Meaning of the Question

Compute the Jacobian of polar coordinates J, then invert the relations to get r and θ in terms of x and y, compute the inverse Jacobian J', and verify that their product equals 1.

C. Basic Concept

The Jacobian of an inverse transformation is the reciprocal of the original Jacobian: [∂(u, v)/∂(x, y)] · [∂(x, y)/∂(u, v)] = 1, analogous to (dy/dx) · (dx/dy) = 1 in single-variable calculus.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: J · J' = [∂(x, y)/∂(r, θ)] · [∂(r, θ)/∂(x, y)] = 1.

Explanation & Conditions: Follows from chain rule of matrix determinants: det(A · A⁻¹) = det(I) = 1.

E. Given Information

x = r cos θ, y = r sin θ ⇒ r = √(x² + y²), θ = tan⁻¹(y/x).

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Recall J = ∂(x, y)/∂(r, θ)
From Question 4, J = r.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: recall j = ∂(x, y)/∂(r, θ) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Express r and θ in terms of x and y
r² = x² + y² ⇒ r = √(x² + y²)
tan θ = y/x ⇒ θ = tan⁻¹(y/x).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: express r and θ in terms of x and y to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Compute partial derivatives of r
∂r/∂x = (1 / 2√(x²+y²)) · 2x = x/r = cos θ
∂r/∂y = (1 / 2√(x²+y²)) · 2y = y/r = sin θ.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: compute partial derivatives of r to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Compute partial derivatives of θ
∂θ/∂x = [1 / (1 + (y/x)²)] · (-y/x²) = -y / (x² + y²) = -sin θ / r
∂θ/∂y = [1 / (1 + (y/x)²)] · (1/x) = x / (x² + y²) = cos θ / r.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: compute partial derivatives of θ to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 5: Compute J' = ∂(r, θ)/∂(x, y)
J' = | ∂r/∂x ∂r/∂y | / | ∂θ/∂x ∂θ/∂y |
= (cos θ)(cos θ / r) - (sin θ)(-sin θ / r)
= (cos² θ + sin² θ) / r = 1/r.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 5: compute j' = ∂(r, θ)/∂(x, y) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 6: Verify J · J' = 1
J · J' = (r) · (1/r) = 1. Hence verified.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 6: verify j · j' = 1 to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: J = r, J' = 1/r, and J · J' = r · (1/r) = 1 (Verified)
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • State both forward and reverse relations.
  • Show step-by-step differentiation of r and θ.
  • Compute determinant and conclude product is 1.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Derivative of tan⁻¹(y/x) with respect to x missing minus sign.
✓ How to Avoid: d(1/x)/dx = -1/x², so a minus sign must appear in ∂θ/∂x.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Forgetting to simplify x/(x²+y²) into cos θ / r.
✓ How to Avoid: Converting to polar coordinates simplifies the determinant algebra enormously.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 8: If u = x + y and v = x - y, find J and J' and verify J · J' = 1.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).

QUESTION 9: Maclaurin Series Expansion of Two Variables: eˣ cos y
Intermediate
A. Full Question:
Expand f(x, y) = eˣ cos y in a Maclaurin series up to second-degree terms.
B. Meaning of the Question

Find the Taylor series expansion of f(x, y) around the origin (0, 0) up to terms of degree 2.

C. Basic Concept

Maclaurin series for two variables approximates any smooth function near (0, 0) by a polynomial in powers of x and y using partial derivatives evaluated at the origin.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: f(x, y) = f(0,0) + [x fx + y fy] + (1/2!)[x² fxx + 2xy fxy + y² fyy] + ... evaluated at (0, 0).

Explanation & Conditions: The 1st bracket gives linear terms; 2nd bracket gives quadratic terms.

E. Given Information

f(x, y) = eˣ cos y, expansion point (a, b) = (0, 0).

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Evaluate f(0, 0)
f(0, 0) = e⁰ cos 0 = 1 · 1 = 1.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: evaluate f(0, 0) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Compute first derivatives and evaluate at (0, 0)
fx = ∂f/∂x = eˣ cos y ⇒ fx(0, 0) = e⁰ cos 0 = 1.
fy = ∂f/∂y = -eˣ sin y ⇒ fy(0, 0) = -e⁰ sin 0 = 0.
💡 Why this step is performed: Set the first partial derivatives fx = 0 and fy = 0 to solve for all stationary (critical) points.
Step 3: Compute second derivatives and evaluate at (0, 0)
fxx = ∂²f/∂x² = eˣ cos y ⇒ fxx(0, 0) = 1.
fxy = ∂²f/∂x∂y = -eˣ sin y ⇒ fxy(0, 0) = 0.
fyy = ∂²f/∂y² = -eˣ cos y ⇒ fyy(0, 0) = -1.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: compute second derivatives and evaluate at (0, 0) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Substitute into Maclaurin series formula
f(x, y) = 1 + [x(1) + y(0)] + (1/2)[x²(1) + 2xy(0) + y²(-1)]
= 1 + x + (1/2)(x² - y²)
= 1 + x + (1/2)x² - (1/2)y².
💡 Why this step is performed: Substitute the evaluated derivative values into the multivariable Taylor/Maclaurin series formula and group terms by degree.
✓ G. Final Answer: eˣ cos y = 1 + x + (1/2)x² - (1/2)y² + ... (up to 2nd degree)
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Write general two-variable Maclaurin formula.
  • Create a neat table of derivatives at (0, 0).
  • Substitute carefully and box answer.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Derivative of cos y taken as +sin y.
✓ How to Avoid: d(cos y)/dy = -sin y, which brings a minus sign into fyy.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Forgetting the factor 1/2! = 1/2 on second-order terms.
✓ How to Avoid: All 2nd degree terms are divided by 2!.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 9: Expand f(x, y) = eˣ sin y in powers of x and y up to second-degree terms.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 1
QUESTION 10: Taylor Series Expansion Around a Non-Zero Point (1, 1)
Intermediate
A. Full Question:
Expand f(x, y) = x²y + 3y - 2 in powers of (x - 1) and (y - 1) up to second-degree terms.
B. Meaning of the Question

Expand the polynomial f(x, y) in terms of powers of (x - 1) and (y - 1) using Taylor's theorem around the point (a, b) = (1, 1).

C. Basic Concept

Taylor series around (a, b) replaces x by (x - a) and y by (y - b), with derivatives evaluated at (a, b).

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: f(x, y) = f(a, b) + [(x-a)fx + (y-b)fy] + (1/2!)[(x-a)²fxx + 2(x-a)(y-b)fxy + (y-b)²fyy] + ...

Explanation & Conditions: (a, b) = (1, 1); h = x - 1, k = y - 1.

E. Given Information

f(x, y) = x²y + 3y - 2, point (a, b) = (1, 1).

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Evaluate f(1, 1)
f(1, 1) = (1)²(1) + 3(1) - 2 = 1 + 3 - 2 = 2.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: evaluate f(1, 1) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: First-order derivatives at (1, 1)
fx = 2xy ⇒ fx(1, 1) = 2(1)(1) = 2.
fy = x² + 3 ⇒ fy(1, 1) = (1)² + 3 = 4.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: first-order derivatives at (1, 1) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Second-order derivatives at (1, 1)
fxx = 2y ⇒ fxx(1, 1) = 2(1) = 2.
fxy = 2x ⇒ fxy(1, 1) = 2(1) = 2.
fyy = 0 ⇒ fyy(1, 1) = 0.
💡 Why this step is performed: Calculate the second-order partial derivatives r = fxx, s = fxy, and t = fyy to prepare for the curvature test.
Step 4: Substitute into Taylor's formula
f(x, y) = 2 + [(x - 1)(2) + (y - 1)(4)] + (1/2)[(x - 1)²(2) + 2(x - 1)(y - 1)(2) + (y - 1)²(0)]
= 2 + 2(x - 1) + 4(y - 1) + (x - 1)² + 2(x - 1)(y - 1).
💡 Why this step is performed: Substitute the evaluated derivative values into the multivariable Taylor/Maclaurin series formula and group terms by degree.
✓ G. Final Answer: f(x, y) = 2 + 2(x - 1) + 4(y - 1) + (x - 1)² + 2(x - 1)(y - 1)
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • State Taylor's formula around (a, b).
  • Show all derivatives evaluated at (1, 1).
  • Keep terms in powers of (x - 1) and (y - 1) without expanding them.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Expanding (x - 1)² into x² - 2x + 1.
✓ How to Avoid: The question asks for expansion IN POWERS of (x - 1) and (y - 1), so do NOT expand the brackets!
⚠️ Common Mistake: Evaluating derivatives at (0, 0) instead of (1, 1).
✓ How to Avoid: Check the expansion point carefully.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 10: Expand f(x, y) = x² + xy + y² in powers of (x - 1) and (y - 2) up to second degree.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).

QUESTION 11: Jacobian for Functional Dependence and Relationship
Intermediate
A. Full Question:
Show that the functions u = (x + y)/(1 - xy) and v = tan⁻¹ x + tan⁻¹ y are functionally dependent, and find the relationship between them.
B. Meaning of the Question

Two functions u and v are functionally dependent if their Jacobian J = ∂(u, v)/∂(x, y) is identically zero. If dependent, find the equation connecting u and v.

C. Basic Concept

If ∂(u, v)/∂(x, y) = 0 identically, there exists a functional relation F(u, v) = 0.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: J = (∂u/∂x)(∂v/∂y) - (∂u/∂y)(∂v/∂x) = 0.

Explanation & Conditions: Identically vanishing Jacobian proves functional dependence.

E. Given Information

u = (x + y)/(1 - xy), v = tan⁻¹ x + tan⁻¹ y.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Compute partial derivatives of u
∂u/∂x = [(1 - xy)(1) - (x + y)(-y)] / (1 - xy)² = [1 - xy + xy + y²] / (1 - xy)² = (1 + y²) / (1 - xy)².
By symmetry: ∂u/∂y = (1 + x²) / (1 - xy)².
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: compute partial derivatives of u to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Compute partial derivatives of v
∂v/∂x = 1 / (1 + x²)
∂v/∂y = 1 / (1 + y²).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: compute partial derivatives of v to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Evaluate the Jacobian determinant J
J = (∂u/∂x)(∂v/∂y) - (∂u/∂y)(∂v/∂x)
= [(1 + y²)/(1 - xy)²] · [1/(1 + y²)] - [(1 + x²)/(1 - xy)²] · [1/(1 + x²)]
= 1/(1 - xy)² - 1/(1 - xy)² = 0.
💡 Why this step is performed: Construct the Jacobian determinant of partial derivatives and simplify using standard trigonometric/algebraic identities.
Step 4: Find the functional relationship
Recall standard trigonometric identity: tan⁻¹ x + tan⁻¹ y = tan⁻¹[(x + y)/(1 - xy)].
Since v = tan⁻¹ x + tan⁻¹ y and u = (x + y)/(1 - xy):
v = tan⁻¹ u or u = tan v.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: find the functional relationship to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: J = ∂(u, v)/∂(x, y) = 0 (Functionally Dependent); Relationship: u = tan v (or v = tan⁻¹ u)
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • State theorem: J = 0 implies functional dependence.
  • Show cancellation of (1+y²) and (1+x²).
  • Write the relationship clearly.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Not simplifying terms before taking determinant.
✓ How to Avoid: Cancel (1+y²) with 1/(1+y²) to avoid messy expressions.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Forgetting to find the relationship.
✓ How to Avoid: Both proving J = 0 and writing u = tan v are required.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 11: Show that u = x + y and v = x/(x + y) are functionally dependent and find the relation.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 1
QUESTION 12: Stationary Points and Extrema of a Two-Variable Cubic
Intermediate
A. Full Question:
Find the extreme values of f(x, y) = x³ + y³ - 3xy.
B. Meaning of the Question

Locate all stationary points of f(x, y) by setting fx = 0, fy = 0, then use D = rt - s² to find local maxima and minima.

C. Basic Concept

A two-variable function can have multiple critical points. Each must be tested individually using the second derivative test.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: fx = 3x² - 3y = 0, fy = 3y² - 3x = 0; D = rt - s².

Explanation & Conditions: r = fxx, s = fxy, t = fyy.

E. Given Information

f(x, y) = x³ + y³ - 3xy.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Set first derivatives to zero
fx = 3x² - 3y = 0 ⇒ y = x²
fy = 3y² - 3x = 0 ⇒ x = y².
Substitute y = x² into second equation:
x = (x²)² = x⁴ ⇒ x⁴ - x = 0 ⇒ x(x³ - 1) = 0.
Real roots: x = 0 or x = 1.
• If x = 0 ⇒ y = 0² = 0. Point: (0, 0).
• If x = 1 ⇒ y = 1² = 1. Point: (1, 1).
Two stationary points: (0, 0) and (1, 1).
💡 Why this step is performed: Set the first partial derivatives fx = 0 and fy = 0 to solve for all stationary (critical) points.
Step 2: Second partial derivatives expressions
r = fxx = 6x, s = fxy = -3, t = fyy = 6y.
Discriminant: D(x, y) = rt - s² = (6x)(6y) - (-3)² = 36xy - 9.
💡 Why this step is performed: Calculate the second-order partial derivatives r = fxx, s = fxy, and t = fyy to prepare for the curvature test.
Step 3: Test Point (0, 0)
At (0, 0): r = 0, s = -3, t = 0.
D = 36(0)(0) - 9 = -9 < 0.
Since D < 0, (0, 0) is a SADDLE POINT (neither maximum nor minimum).
💡 Why this step is performed: Evaluate the Hessian discriminant D = rt - s²; D > 0 and r > 0 indicates a local minimum, while D > 0 and r < 0 indicates a local maximum.
Step 4: Test Point (1, 1)
At (1, 1): r = 6(1) = 6, s = -3, t = 6(1) = 6.
D = 36(1)(1) - 9 = 27 > 0 and r = 6 > 0.
Since D > 0 and r > 0, (1, 1) is a LOCAL MINIMUM.
Minimum value: f(1, 1) = 1³ + 1³ - 3(1)(1) = 1 + 1 - 3 = -1.
💡 Why this step is performed: Evaluate the Hessian discriminant D = rt - s²; D > 0 and r > 0 indicates a local minimum, while D > 0 and r < 0 indicates a local maximum.
✓ G. Final Answer: At (0, 0): Saddle point. At (1, 1): Local Minimum with minimum value f_min = -1.
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Solve non-linear system x = y², y = x² clearly.
  • List both stationary points in a table.
  • Evaluate D and r at each point clearly.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Dividing by x in x⁴ = x and losing the root x = 0.
✓ How to Avoid: Never divide by the variable! Factorize as x(x³ - 1) = 0.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Classifying (0, 0) as minimum because f(0,0)=0 > -1.
✓ How to Avoid: Local nature is determined ONLY by the sign of D, not function value!
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 12: Find the stationary points and extreme values of f(x, y) = x³ + 3xy² - 15x² - 15y² + 72x.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).

QUESTION 13: Jacobian of 3-Variable Spherical Polar Coordinates
Intermediate
A. Full Question:
If x = r sin θ cos φ, y = r sin θ sin φ, z = r cos θ, evaluate J = ∂(x, y, z)/∂(r, θ, φ).
B. Meaning of the Question

Calculate the 3×3 Jacobian determinant of the transformation from Cartesian coordinates (x, y, z) to spherical coordinates (r, θ, φ).

C. Basic Concept

The spherical Jacobian represents the volume differential element in triple integration: dx dy dz = r² sin θ dr dθ dφ.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: J = det [∂(x, y, z)/∂(r, θ, φ)].

Explanation & Conditions: Row 1: [∂x/∂r, ∂x/∂θ, ∂x/∂φ], Row 2: [∂y/∂r, ∂y/∂θ, ∂y/∂φ], Row 3: [∂z/∂r, ∂z/∂θ, ∂z/∂φ].

E. Given Information

x = r sin θ cos φ, y = r sin θ sin φ, z = r cos θ.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Compute partial derivatives for Row 1 (x)
∂x/∂r = sin θ cos φ
∂x/∂θ = r cos θ cos φ
∂x/∂φ = -r sin θ sin φ.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: compute partial derivatives for row 1 (x) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Compute partial derivatives for Row 2 (y)
∂y/∂r = sin θ sin φ
∂y/∂θ = r cos θ sin φ
∂y/∂φ = r sin θ cos φ.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: compute partial derivatives for row 2 (y) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Compute partial derivatives for Row 3 (z)
∂z/∂r = cos θ
∂z/∂θ = -r sin θ
∂z/∂φ = 0 (since z has no φ).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: compute partial derivatives for row 3 (z) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Expand determinant along Row 3
J = cos θ · | r cos θ cos φ -r sin θ sin φ |
| r cos θ sin φ r sin θ cos φ |
- (-r sin θ) · | sin θ cos φ -r sin θ sin φ |
| sin θ sin φ r sin θ cos φ | + 0.
= cos θ [ r² sin θ cos θ (cos² φ + sin² φ) ] + r sin θ [ r sin² θ (cos² φ + sin² φ) ]
= cos θ [ r² sin θ cos θ (1) ] + r sin θ [ r sin² θ (1) ]
= r² sin θ cos² θ + r² sin³ θ
= r² sin θ (cos² θ + sin² θ) = r² sin θ (1) = r² sin θ.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: expand determinant along row 3 to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: J = ∂(x, y, z)/∂(r, θ, φ) = r² sin θ
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Write out the full 3×3 matrix.
  • Expand along the 3rd row because it contains a zero.
  • Factor out r² sin θ.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Expanding along Row 1.
✓ How to Avoid: Expanding along Row 3 is much faster because ∂z/∂φ = 0.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Sign error with (-r sin θ).
✓ How to Avoid: The (3, 2) cofactor position has a minus sign: -(-r sin θ) = +r sin θ.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 13: If x = ρ cos φ, y = ρ sin φ, z = z, find the Jacobian ∂(x, y, z)/∂(ρ, φ, z) in cylindrical coordinates.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 1
QUESTION 14: Homogeneous Function of Degree Zero: u = tan⁻¹(y/x)
Intermediate
A. Full Question:
If u = tan⁻¹(y/x), show that x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y) = 0 and x²(∂²u/∂x²) + 2xy(∂²u/∂x∂y) + y²(∂²u/∂y²) = 0.
B. Meaning of the Question

Show that u is homogeneous of degree 0, then verify both the first and second-order forms of Euler's Theorem.

C. Basic Concept

When a function has equal powers in numerator and denominator, its degree is n = 0. Euler's second-order theorem states: x² u_xx + 2xy u_xy + y² u_yy = n(n - 1)u.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: First-order: x u_x + y u_y = n u = 0·u = 0.
Second-order: x² u_xx + 2xy u_xy + y² u_yy = n(n - 1)u = 0(-1)u = 0.

Explanation & Conditions: For n = 0, both n·u and n(n - 1)u vanish identically.

E. Given Information

u = tan⁻¹(y/x).

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Check degree of homogeneity
u(tx, ty) = tan⁻¹(ty / tx) = tan⁻¹(y / x) = t⁰ u(x, y).
Therefore, u is a homogeneous function of degree n = 0.
💡 Why this step is performed: Substitute tx and ty for x and y to factor out t^n and establish the degree of homogeneity n.
Step 2: Compute first derivatives
∂u/∂x = [1 / (1 + (y/x)²)] · (-y/x²) = -y / (x² + y²).
∂u/∂y = [1 / (1 + (y/x)²)] · (1/x) = x / (x² + y²).
💡 Why this step is performed: Set the first partial derivatives fx = 0 and fy = 0 to solve for all stationary (critical) points.
Step 3: Verify first-order Euler theorem
x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y) = x[-y/(x²+y²)] + y[x/(x²+y²)] = (-xy + xy)/(x²+y²) = 0.
Matches n·u = 0 · u = 0. Verified!
💡 Why this step is performed: Combine the partial derivatives into the linear combination x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y) and verify it equals n·u.
Step 4: Verify second-order Euler theorem
By Euler's 2nd-order theorem:
x² u_xx + 2xy u_xy + y² u_yy = n(n - 1) u.
Since n = 0:
n(n - 1) u = 0(0 - 1) u = 0. Hence proved.
💡 Why this step is performed: Apply Euler's second-order theorem x²r + 2xys + y²t = n(n-1)u to evaluate the quadratic derivative form.
✓ G. Final Answer: x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y) = 0 and x²(∂²u/∂x²) + 2xy(∂²u/∂x∂y) + y²(∂²u/∂y²) = 0 (Proved)
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • State n = 0 clearly from u(tx, ty) = t⁰ u.
  • Show cancellation for first-order directly.
  • Quote Euler's second-order theorem x²r + 2xys + y²t = n(n-1)u.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Thinking degree is undefined.
✓ How to Avoid: t cancels completely, meaning t⁰ = 1, so degree is 0, not undefined.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Long calculations for 2nd derivatives.
✓ How to Avoid: Using Euler's theorem n(n-1)u saves two pages of tedious algebra.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 14: If u = log[(x² + y²)/(xy)], show that x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y) = 0.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 1
QUESTION 15: Composite Partial Differentiation (Chain Rule with Intermediate Variables)
Intermediate
A. Full Question:
If z = f(u, v) where u = x² - y² and v = 2xy, prove that y(∂z/∂x) + x(∂z/∂y) = 2(x² + y²)(∂z/∂v).
B. Meaning of the Question

Use the multivariable chain rule to express ∂z/∂x and ∂z/∂y in terms of ∂z/∂u and ∂z/∂v, then compute the linear combination y(∂z/∂x) + x(∂z/∂y).

C. Basic Concept

When z = f(u, v), ∂z/∂x = (∂z/∂u)(∂u/∂x) + (∂z/∂v)(∂v/∂x) and ∂z/∂y = (∂z/∂u)(∂u/∂y) + (∂z/∂v)(∂v/∂y).

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: ∂z/∂x = f_u u_x + f_v v_x; ∂z/∂y = f_u u_y + f_v v_y.

Explanation & Conditions: u_x = 2x, u_y = -2y, v_x = 2y, v_y = 2x.

E. Given Information

z = f(u, v), u = x² - y², v = 2xy.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Compute derivatives of u and v
∂u/∂x = 2x, ∂u/∂y = -2y
∂v/∂x = 2y, ∂v/∂y = 2x.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: compute derivatives of u and v to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Express ∂z/∂x using chain rule
∂z/∂x = (∂z/∂u)(2x) + (∂z/∂v)(2y) = 2x(∂z/∂u) + 2y(∂z/∂v).
Multiply by y: y(∂z/∂x) = 2xy(∂z/∂u) + 2y²(∂z/∂v).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: express ∂z/∂x using chain rule to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Express ∂z/∂y using chain rule
∂z/∂y = (∂z/∂u)(-2y) + (∂z/∂v)(2x) = -2y(∂z/∂u) + 2x(∂z/∂v).
Multiply by x: x(∂z/∂y) = -2xy(∂z/∂u) + 2x²(∂z/∂v).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: express ∂z/∂y using chain rule to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Add the two equations
LHS = y(∂z/∂x) + x(∂z/∂y)
= [2xy(∂z/∂u) - 2xy(∂z/∂u)] + [2y²(∂z/∂v) + 2x²(∂z/∂v)]
= 0 + 2(x² + y²)(∂z/∂v)
= 2(x² + y²)(∂z/∂v) = RHS. Hence proved.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: add the two equations to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: y(∂z/∂x) + x(∂z/∂y) = 2(x² + y²)(∂z/∂v) (Hence proved)
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Write chain rule formulas clearly.
  • Show the multiplier y and x on each line.
  • Highlight the cancellation of the ∂z/∂u terms.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Sign error: ∂u/∂y = +2y instead of -2y.
✓ How to Avoid: d(-y²)/dy = -2y. The minus sign is vital for cancellation!
⚠️ Common Mistake: Treating z as a product instead of general function f(u, v).
✓ How to Avoid: Keep ∂z/∂u and ∂z/∂v as operators.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 15: If z = f(x - y, y - z), show that ∂z/∂x + ∂z/∂y + ∂z/∂z = 0.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 1
QUESTION 16: Euler's Theorem on Inverse Tangent: x u_x + y u_y = sin 2u
Exam-Level
A. Full Question:
If u = tan⁻¹[(x³ + y³) / (x - y)], prove that x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y) = sin 2u.
B. Meaning of the Question

Express tan u as a homogeneous function of degree 2, apply Euler's Theorem, and use trigonometric identity to prove the result equals sin 2u.

C. Basic Concept

Standard VTU exam problem. Let z = tan u = f(x, y). Since f is homogeneous of degree n = 3 - 1 = 2, Euler's theorem gives x z_x + y z_y = 2z. Then using chain rule z_x = sec² u · u_x yields 2 tan u / sec² u = 2 sin u cos u = sin 2u.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y) = n [f(u) / f'(u)] = 2 [tan u / sec² u] = sin 2u.

Explanation & Conditions: tan u = sin u / cos u; dividing by sec² u = 1/cos² u gives sin u cos u.

E. Given Information

u = tan⁻¹[(x³ + y³) / (x - y)].

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Define z = tan u
tan u = (x³ + y³) / (x - y) = z(x, y).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: define z = tan u to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Find degree of z
z(tx, ty) = [(tx)³ + (ty)³] / [tx - ty] = t³(x³ + y³) / [t(x - y)] = t² z(x, y).
Therefore, z is homogeneous of degree n = 3 - 1 = 2.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: find degree of z to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Apply Euler's Theorem to z
x(∂z/∂x) + y(∂z/∂y) = n · z = 2z = 2 tan u.
💡 Why this step is performed: Combine the partial derivatives into the linear combination x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y) and verify it equals n·u.
Step 4: Express derivatives in terms of u
∂z/∂x = sec² u (∂u/∂x), ∂z/∂y = sec² u (∂u/∂y).
Substitute into Euler equation:
x [sec² u (∂u/∂x)] + y [sec² u (∂u/∂y)] = 2 tan u
sec² u [x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y)] = 2 tan u.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: express derivatives in terms of u to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 5: Divide by sec² u and simplify with trigonometry
x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y) = 2 tan u / sec² u
= 2 (sin u / cos u) · cos² u = 2 sin u cos u = sin 2u. Hence proved.
💡 Why this step is performed: Compute the sum of partial derivatives of the component functions along their respective coordinate axes.
✓ G. Final Answer: x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y) = sin 2u (Hence proved)
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • State z = tan u is homogeneous of degree 2.
  • Write Euler's theorem on z clearly.
  • Show trigonometric conversion: 2 tan u / sec² u = 2 sin u cos u = sin 2u.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Saying u is homogeneous of degree 2.
✓ How to Avoid: u is NOT homogeneous. tan u is homogeneous!
⚠️ Common Mistake: Leaving answer as 2 tan u / sec² u.
✓ How to Avoid: Always simplify to the standard double-angle identity sin 2u.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 16: If u = tan⁻¹[(x² + y²) / (x + y)], prove that x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y) = (1/2) sin 2u.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 1
QUESTION 17: Second-Order Euler's Theorem on Inverse Tangent
Exam-Level
A. Full Question:
If u = tan⁻¹[(x³ + y³) / (x - y)], prove that x²(∂²u/∂x²) + 2xy(∂²u/∂x∂y) + y²(∂²u/∂y²) = (1 - 4 sin² u) sin 2u / ... = 2 cos 3u sin u (or standard form).
B. Meaning of the Question

Differentiate Euler's first-order result partially with respect to x and y, combine, and obtain the second-order partial differential relation.

C. Basic Concept

Let G(u) = x u_x + y u_y. Then x² u_xx + 2xy u_xy + y² u_yy = G(u)[G'(u) - 1]. Since G(u) = sin 2u, G'(u) = 2 cos 2u, so result is sin 2u (2 cos 2u - 1).

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: x² u_xx + 2xy u_xy + y² u_yy = f(u)[f'(u) - 1] where f(u) = sin 2u.

Explanation & Conditions: f(u) = sin 2u ⇒ f'(u) = 2 cos 2u. Therefore f(u)[f'(u) - 1] = sin 2u (2 cos 2u - 1).

E. Given Information

u = tan⁻¹[(x³ + y³) / (x - y)] ⇒ x u_x + y u_y = sin 2u.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Recall the first-order result
From Question 16: x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y) = sin 2u = f(u).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: recall the first-order result to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Differentiate with respect to x
∂/∂x [x u_x + y u_y] = ∂/∂x [sin 2u]
1 · u_x + x u_xx + y u_yx = 2 cos 2u · u_x
x u_xx + y u_xy = 2 cos 2u · u_x - u_x = (2 cos 2u - 1) u_x.
Multiply by x: x² u_xx + xy u_xy = (2 cos 2u - 1) x u_x.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: differentiate with respect to x to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Differentiate with respect to y
∂/∂y [x u_x + y u_y] = ∂/∂y [sin 2u]
x u_xy + 1 · u_y + y u_yy = 2 cos 2u · u_y
x u_xy + y u_yy = (2 cos 2u - 1) u_y.
Multiply by y: xy u_xy + y² u_yy = (2 cos 2u - 1) y u_y.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: differentiate with respect to y to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Add the two equations
LHS = x² u_xx + 2xy u_xy + y² u_yy
= (2 cos 2u - 1) [x u_x + y u_y]
= (2 cos 2u - 1) sin 2u
= sin 2u (2 cos 2u - 1).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: add the two equations to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 5: Express in alternative trigonometric forms if requested
sin 2u (2 cos 2u - 1) = 2 sin 2u cos 2u - sin 2u = sin 4u - sin 2u
= 2 cos 3u sin u.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 5: express in alternative trigonometric forms if requested to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: x²(∂²u/∂x²) + 2xy(∂²u/∂x∂y) + y²(∂²u/∂y²) = sin 2u (2 cos 2u - 1) = 2 cos 3u sin u
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Show differentiation with respect to x and y step by step.
  • Use Clairaut's theorem u_xy = u_yx.
  • Factor out (2 cos 2u - 1) and substitute x u_x + y u_y = sin 2u.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Forgetting the product rule on x u_x.
✓ How to Avoid: d(x u_x)/dx = 1·u_x + x u_xx.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Forgetting the inner derivative u_x on RHS.
✓ How to Avoid: d(sin 2u)/dx = 2 cos 2u · (∂u/∂x).
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 17: If u = sin⁻¹[(x² + y²)/(x + y)], prove that x² u_xx + 2xy u_xy + y² u_yy = tan u (tan² u - 1) / 4 (or standard form).
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 1
QUESTION 18: Radial Laplace Operator: u = f(r) where r² = x² + y² + z²
Exam-Level
A. Full Question:
If u = f(r) where r² = x² + y² + z², prove that ∂²u/∂x² + ∂²u/∂y² + ∂²u/∂z² = f''(r) + (2/r) f'(r).
B. Meaning of the Question

Compute the 3D Laplacian ∇²u of a radially symmetric function u = f(r) in Cartesian coordinates.

C. Basic Concept

Radial symmetry simplifies 3D PDEs (such as heat and wave equations) into ordinary differential equations in r.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: ∇²u = u_xx + u_yy + u_zz = f''(r) + (2/r) f'(r).

Explanation & Conditions: r_x = x/r, r_xx = (r² - x²)/r³.

E. Given Information

u = f(r), r² = x² + y² + z².

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Compute partial derivatives of r
2r (∂r/∂x) = 2x ⇒ ∂r/∂x = x/r.
Similarly, ∂r/∂y = y/r and ∂r/∂z = z/r.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: compute partial derivatives of r to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Compute first derivative ∂u/∂x
∂u/∂x = f'(r) · (∂r/∂x) = f'(r) · (x/r) = (x/r) f'(r).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: compute first derivative ∂u/∂x to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Compute second derivative ∂²u/∂x² using quotient/product rule
∂²u/∂x² = ∂/∂x [(x/r) f'(r)] = (1/r) f'(r) + x · ∂/∂x [f'(r)/r]
= (1/r) f'(r) + x [ r f''(r)(x/r) - f'(r)(x/r) ] / r²
= (1/r) f'(r) + (x²/r²) f''(r) - (x²/r³) f'(r).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: compute second derivative ∂²u/∂x² using quotient/product rule to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Sum cyclically over x, y, and z
u_xx + u_yy + u_zz = [ 3/r f'(r) ] + [ (x² + y² + z²)/r² ] f''(r) - [ (x² + y² + z²)/r³ ] f'(r).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: sum cyclically over x, y, and z to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 5: Substitute x² + y² + z² = r²
= (3/r) f'(r) + (r²/r²) f''(r) - (r²/r³) f'(r)
= (3/r) f'(r) + f''(r) - (1/r) f'(r)
= f''(r) + (2/r) f'(r). Hence proved.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 5: substitute x² + y² + z² = r² to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: ∂²u/∂x² + ∂²u/∂y² + ∂²u/∂z² = f''(r) + (2/r) f'(r) (Hence proved)
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Show ∂r/∂x = x/r clearly.
  • Show quotient rule for ∂²u/∂x² in full detail.
  • Combine cyclic terms using symmetry.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Treating r as constant.
✓ How to Avoid: r depends on x, y, z through r² = x² + y² + z².
⚠️ Common Mistake: Algebra error in (3/r) - (1/r).
✓ How to Avoid: 3/r - 1/r = 2/r.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 18: Find f(r) such that ∇²f(r) = 0 (harmonic function in 3D).
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 1
QUESTION 19: Taylor Series of Two Variables up to 3rd Degree: eˣ log(1 + y)
Exam-Level
A. Full Question:
Expand f(x, y) = eˣ log(1 + y) in powers of x and y up to third-degree terms.
B. Meaning of the Question

Obtain the Maclaurin expansion of eˣ log(1 + y) around (0, 0) up to terms of combined degree 3.

C. Basic Concept

Can be solved either by computing partial derivatives up to order 3 at (0, 0) or by multiplying the standard series of eˣ and log(1 + y). In exams, showing the partial derivative values guarantees full marks.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: f(x, y) = f(0,0) + [x fx + y fy] + (1/2!)[x² fxx + 2xy fxy + y² fyy] + (1/3!)[x³ fxxx + 3x²y fxxy + 3xy² fxyy + y³ fyyy] + ...

Explanation & Conditions: All derivatives evaluated at (0, 0).

E. Given Information

f(x, y) = eˣ log(1 + y), expansion point (0, 0).

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Function and 1st derivatives at (0, 0)
f(0, 0) = e⁰ log(1) = 0.
fx = eˣ log(1 + y) ⇒ fx(0, 0) = 0.
fy = eˣ / (1 + y) ⇒ fy(0, 0) = 1.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: function and 1st derivatives at (0, 0) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: 2nd-order derivatives at (0, 0)
fxx = eˣ log(1 + y) ⇒ fxx(0, 0) = 0.
fxy = eˣ / (1 + y) ⇒ fxy(0, 0) = 1.
fyy = -eˣ / (1 + y)² ⇒ fyy(0, 0) = -1.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: 2nd-order derivatives at (0, 0) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: 3rd-order derivatives at (0, 0)
fxxx = eˣ log(1 + y) ⇒ fxxx(0, 0) = 0.
fxxy = eˣ / (1 + y) ⇒ fxxy(0, 0) = 1.
fxyy = -eˣ / (1 + y)² ⇒ fxyy(0, 0) = -1.
fyyy = 2eˣ / (1 + y)³ ⇒ fyyy(0, 0) = 2.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: 3rd-order derivatives at (0, 0) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Substitute into Taylor formula
Linear: y.
Quadratic: (1/2)[2xy(1) + y²(-1)] = xy - (1/2)y².
Cubic: (1/6)[3x²y(1) + 3xy²(-1) + y³(2)] = (1/2)x²y - (1/2)xy² + (1/3)y³.
Combine all terms:
f(x, y) = y + xy - (1/2)y² + (1/2)x²y - (1/2)xy² + (1/3)y³ + ...
💡 Why this step is performed: Substitute the evaluated derivative values into the multivariable Taylor/Maclaurin series formula and group terms by degree.
✓ G. Final Answer: eˣ log(1 + y) = y + xy - (1/2)y² + (1/2)x²y - (1/2)xy² + (1/3)y³ + ...
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • List all derivatives systematically by order (1st, 2nd, 3rd).
  • State the multinomial coefficients 1, 3, 3, 1 for the 3rd degree bracket.
  • Group by degree: linear, quadratic, cubic.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Derivative of 1/(1+y)².
✓ How to Avoid: d[-(1+y)⁻²]/dy = +2(1+y)⁻³ = 2/(1+y)³.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Missing 1/3! = 1/6 on third degree terms.
✓ How to Avoid: Always divide by the corresponding factorial.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 19: Expand f(x, y) = e^y log(1 + x) up to third-degree terms.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 1
QUESTION 20: Global / Constrained Maxima and Minima: Dimensions of Open Rectangular Box
Exam-Level
A. Full Question:
A rectangular box open at the top is to have a volume of 32 cubic feet. Find the dimensions of the box requiring the least material for its construction.
B. Meaning of the Question

Minimize the surface area S = xy + 2yz + 2xz subject to the volume constraint V = xyz = 32.

C. Basic Concept

Optimization of two independent variables by eliminating the third variable using the constraint equation.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: Constraint: V = xyz = 32 ⇒ z = 32/(xy).
Surface area: S(x, y) = xy + 2xz + 2yz = xy + 64/y + 64/x.

Explanation & Conditions: xy is the base; 2xz and 2yz are the four open sides.

E. Given Information

Volume V = xyz = 32; Open top surface area S = xy + 2yz + 2xz.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Express S as a function of x and y
z = 32 / (xy).
S(x, y) = xy + 2x(32/xy) + 2y(32/xy) = xy + 64/y + 64/x.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: express s as a function of x and y to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Find stationary points: Sx = 0 and Sy = 0
Sx = y - 64/x² = 0 ⇒ y = 64/x².
Sy = x - 64/y² = 0 ⇒ x = 64/y².
Substitute y into x equation:
x = 64 / (64/x²)² = 64 · (x⁴ / 4096) = x⁴ / 64
x⁴ - 64x = 0 ⇒ x(x³ - 64) = 0.
Since x > 0 (dimension of box): x³ = 64 ⇒ x = 4.
Then y = 64 / 4² = 64 / 16 = 4.
💡 Why this step is performed: Set the first partial derivatives fx = 0 and fy = 0 to solve for all stationary (critical) points.
Step 3: Second derivative test at (4, 4)
r = Sxx = 128/x³ ⇒ r(4, 4) = 128/64 = 2.
s = Sxy = 1.
t = Syy = 128/y³ ⇒ t(4, 4) = 128/64 = 2.
D = rt - s² = (2)(2) - 1² = 4 - 1 = 3 > 0 and r = 2 > 0.
Thus, S has a LOCAL MINIMUM at (4, 4).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: second derivative test at (4, 4) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Find height z and minimum surface area
z = 32 / (xy) = 32 / (4 · 4) = 32 / 16 = 2 feet.
S_min = (4)(4) + 64/4 + 64/4 = 16 + 16 + 16 = 48 sq. ft.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: find height z and minimum surface area to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: Dimensions: Length x = 4 ft, Breadth y = 4 ft, Height z = 2 ft (Minimum Surface Area = 48 sq. ft)
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • State constraint and surface area formulas clearly.
  • Eliminate z and write S(x, y).
  • Verify D > 0 and r > 0 to prove it is a minimum.
  • State all 3 dimensions with units.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Including 2xy for closed box.
✓ How to Avoid: The box is OPEN AT THE TOP, so only one base xy is included!
⚠️ Common Mistake: Forgetting to calculate the 3rd dimension z.
✓ How to Avoid: Always calculate z = 32/(xy).
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 20: Find dimensions of an open rectangular box of volume 108 cubic cm requiring least material.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 1
QUESTION 21: Stationary Points and Nature: f(x, y) = sin x + sin y + sin(x + y)
Exam-Level
A. Full Question:
Find the maximum and minimum values of f(x, y) = sin x + sin y + sin(x + y) in the range 0 < x, y < π.
B. Meaning of the Question

Find the critical points of the trigonometric function f(x, y) in the first quadrant and determine where it attains its maximum value.

C. Basic Concept

Classic VTU exam problem related to maximum area of a triangle. Uses trigonometric identities cos A - cos B = -2 sin[(A+B)/2] sin[(A-B)/2].

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: fx = cos x + cos(x + y) = 0, fy = cos y + cos(x + y) = 0.

Explanation & Conditions: Subtracting gives cos x - cos y = 0 ⇒ x = y in (0, π).

E. Given Information

f(x, y) = sin x + sin y + sin(x + y), 0 < x, y < π.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Set first derivatives to zero
fx = cos x + cos(x + y) = 0 --- (1)
fy = cos y + cos(x + y) = 0 --- (2).
💡 Why this step is performed: Set the first partial derivatives fx = 0 and fy = 0 to solve for all stationary (critical) points.
Step 2: Solve the system of trigonometric equations
Subtracting (2) from (1):
cos x - cos y = 0 ⇒ cos x = cos y.
Since 0 < x, y < π, this implies x = y.
Substitute x = y into (1):
cos x + cos(2x) = 0 ⇒ cos 2x = -cos x = cos(π - x).
2x = π - x ⇒ 3x = π ⇒ x = π/3.
Since x = y, y = π/3.
Critical point: (π/3, π/3).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: solve the system of trigonometric equations to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Compute second derivatives at (π/3, π/3)
r = fxx = -sin x - sin(x + y) ⇒ r(π/3, π/3) = -sin(π/3) - sin(2π/3) = -√3/2 - √3/2 = -√3.
s = fxy = -sin(x + y) ⇒ s(π/3, π/3) = -sin(2π/3) = -√3/2.
t = fyy = -sin y - sin(x + y) ⇒ t(π/3, π/3) = -√3.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: compute second derivatives at (π/3, π/3) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Evaluate D = rt - s²
D = (-√3)(-√3) - (-√3/2)² = 3 - 3/4 = 9/4 > 0.
Since D > 0 and r = -√3 < 0, f has a LOCAL MAXIMUM at (π/3, π/3).
💡 Why this step is performed: Evaluate the Hessian discriminant D = rt - s²; D > 0 and r > 0 indicates a local minimum, while D > 0 and r < 0 indicates a local maximum.
Step 5: Compute Maximum Value
f_max = sin(π/3) + sin(π/3) + sin(2π/3) = √3/2 + √3/2 + √3/2 = (3√3)/2.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 5: compute maximum value to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: Local Maximum at (π/3, π/3) with Maximum Value f_max = (3√3)/2
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Show cos x = cos y gives x = y.
  • Show cos 2x = -cos x = cos(π - x) gives x = π/3.
  • State D = 9/4 > 0 and r < 0 implies Maximum.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Concluding Minimum because of negative signs in r.
✓ How to Avoid: r < 0 means concave downwards, which is a MAXIMUM!
⚠️ Common Mistake: Value of sin(2π/3) written as -√3/2.
✓ How to Avoid: 2π/3 is in Quadrant II where sine is POSITIVE: sin(2π/3) = +√3/2.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 21: Find the maximum value of f(x, y) = cos x + cos y + cos(x + y) for 0 < x, y < π/2.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 1
QUESTION 22: Jacobian of Implicit Functions
Exam-Level
A. Full Question:
If u³ + v + w = x + y² + z², u + v³ + w = x² + y + z², u + v + w³ = x² + y² + z, find ∂u/∂x.
B. Meaning of the Question

Use the Jacobian formula for implicit functions to find the partial derivative ∂u/∂x when u, v, w are implicitly defined functions of x, y, z.

C. Basic Concept

For a system of implicit equations F₁(u,v,w,x,y,z) = 0, F₂ = 0, F₃ = 0: ∂u/∂x = - [∂(F₁, F₂, F₃)/∂(x, v, w)] / [∂(F₁, F₂, F₃)/∂(u, v, w)].

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: ∂u/∂x = - J(x, v, w) / J(u, v, w).

Explanation & Conditions: Denominator is the Jacobian with respect to dependent variables; numerator replaces u by x.

E. Given Information

F₁ = u³ + v + w - x - y² - z² = 0, F₂ = u + v³ + w - x² - y - z² = 0, F₃ = u + v + w³ - x² - y² - z = 0.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Compute Denominator Jacobian J(u, v, w)
J(u, v, w) = | 3u² 1 1 |
| 1 3v² 1 |
| 1 1 3w² |.
Row operations R₂ → R₂ - R₁, R₃ → R₃ - R₁:
J(u, v, w) = | 3u² 1 1 |
| 1-3u² 3v²-1 0 |
| 1-3u² 0 3w²-1 |.
Evaluating this gives: 27u²v²w² - 3(u² + v² + w²) + 2.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: compute denominator jacobian j(u, v, w) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Compute Numerator Jacobian J(x, v, w)
J(x, v, w) = | ∂F₁/∂x ∂F₁/∂v ∂F₁/∂w | = | -1 1 1 |
| ∂F₂/∂x ∂F₂/∂v ∂F₂/∂w | | -2x 3v² 1 |
| ∂F₃/∂x ∂F₃/∂v ∂F₃/∂w | | -2x 1 3w² |.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: compute numerator jacobian j(x, v, w) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Evaluate Numerator Determinant
Expanding along column 1:
= -1 [ 9v²w² - 1 ] - (-2x) [ 3w² - 1 ] + (-2x) [ 1 - 3v² ]
= 1 - 9v²w² + 2x(3w² - 1 - 1 + 3v²)
= 1 - 9v²w² + 2x[3(v² + w²) - 2].
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: evaluate numerator determinant to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Combine using implicit differentiation formula
∂u/∂x = - [J(x, v, w)] / [J(u, v, w)].
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: combine using implicit differentiation formula to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: ∂u/∂x = - [ 1 - 9v²w² + 2x(3v² + 3w² - 2) ] / [ 27u²v²w² - 3(u² + v² + w²) + 2 ]
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • State the implicit Jacobian ratio formula with minus sign.
  • Evaluate both 3×3 determinants separately.
  • Substitute into final quotient.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Forgetting the leading negative sign in implicit derivative formula.
✓ How to Avoid: dy/dx = - Fx / Fy always has a minus sign!
⚠️ Common Mistake: Replacing the wrong column.
✓ How to Avoid: To find ∂u/∂x, replace the 'u' column with 'x' derivatives.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 22: If u + v + w = x + y + z, u² + v² + w² = x² + y² + z², u³ + v³ + w³ = x³ + y³ + z³, find ∂u/∂x.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 1
QUESTION 23: Lagrange's Method of Multipliers: Minimum Distance to a Plane
Exam-Level
A. Full Question:
Find the shortest distance from the origin to the plane x + 2y + 2z = 9 using Lagrange's method of undetermined multipliers.
B. Meaning of the Question

Minimize the square of distance f(x, y, z) = x² + y² + z² subject to the plane constraint g(x, y, z) = x + 2y + 2z - 9 = 0.

C. Basic Concept

Lagrange function: L(x, y, z, λ) = f + λg. At extrema, ∇f = -λ ∇g, meaning the gradient of the objective function is parallel to the normal vector of the constraint surface.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: L = (x² + y² + z²) + λ(x + 2y + 2z - 9). Conditions: Lx = 0, Ly = 0, Lz = 0, Lλ = 0.

Explanation & Conditions: Minimizing x² + y² + z² is mathematically equivalent to minimizing the square root distance, but avoids radical algebra.

E. Given Information

Objective: f = x² + y² + z²; Constraint: x + 2y + 2z = 9.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Set up Lagrange equations
Lx = 2x + λ = 0 ⇒ x = -λ/2.
Ly = 2y + 2λ = 0 ⇒ y = -λ.
Lz = 2z + 2λ = 0 ⇒ z = -λ.
💡 Why this step is performed: Set up the auxiliary Lagrange function L = f + λg and set all first partial derivatives to zero.
Step 2: Substitute x, y, z into the constraint equation
x + 2y + 2z = 9
(-λ/2) + 2(-λ) + 2(-λ) = 9
-λ/2 - 4λ = 9 ⇒ -9λ/2 = 9 ⇒ λ = -2.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: substitute x, y, z into the constraint equation to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Determine the closest point coordinates
x = -(-2)/2 = 1.
y = -(-2) = 2.
z = -(-2) = 2.
The closest point on the plane is (1, 2, 2).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: determine the closest point coordinates to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Compute the minimum distance
d = √(x² + y² + z²) = √(1² + 2² + 2²) = √(1 + 4 + 4) = √9 = 3 units.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: compute the minimum distance to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: Closest point is (1, 2, 2); Minimum Distance = 3 units
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • State objective function f = x² + y² + z².
  • Form Lagrange function L = f + λg.
  • Solve for λ and then (x, y, z).
  • State distance d = √9 = 3.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Using d = √(x²+y²+z²) as objective function.
✓ How to Avoid: Differentiating square roots is messy. Always minimize f = x² + y² + z² instead!
⚠️ Common Mistake: Arithmetic error in -λ/2 - 4λ.
✓ How to Avoid: -λ/2 - 8λ/2 = -9λ/2.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 23: Find the shortest distance from the origin to the surface xyz = 8 using Lagrange multipliers.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 1
QUESTION 24: Chain Rule Derivative Transformation: Wave Equation Form
Exam-Level
A. Full Question:
If u = f(x - ct) + g(x + ct), prove that ∂²u/∂t² = c² (∂²u/∂x²).
B. Meaning of the Question

Show that any combination of forward and backward travelling waves u = f(x - ct) + g(x + ct) satisfies the one-dimensional classical wave equation.

C. Basic Concept

Fundamental partial differential equation of vibrations and wave propagation (d'Alembert's solution of wave equation).

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: Let v = x - ct, w = x + ct. Then ∂/∂t = -c(d/dv) + c(d/dw) and ∂/∂x = d/dv + d/dw.

Explanation & Conditions: c is the wave propagation velocity.

E. Given Information

u = f(v) + g(w), where v = x - ct, w = x + ct.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Compute first partial derivatives with respect to x
∂u/∂x = f'(v) · (∂v/∂x) + g'(w) · (∂w/∂x) = f'(v)(1) + g'(w)(1) = f'(v) + g'(w).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: compute first partial derivatives with respect to x to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Compute second partial derivative with respect to x
∂²u/∂x² = ∂/∂x [f'(v) + g'(w)] = f''(v)(1) + g''(w)(1) = f''(v) + g''(w).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: compute second partial derivative with respect to x to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Compute first partial derivative with respect to t
∂u/∂t = f'(v) · (∂v/∂t) + g'(w) · (∂w/∂t) = f'(v)(-c) + g'(w)(c) = -c f'(v) + c g'(w).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: compute first partial derivative with respect to t to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Compute second partial derivative with respect to t
∂²u/∂t² = ∂/∂t [-c f'(v) + c g'(w)]
= -c f''(v)(-c) + c g''(w)(c)
= c² f''(v) + c² g''(w) = c² [f''(v) + g''(w)].
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: compute second partial derivative with respect to t to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 5: Substitute Step 2 into Step 4
∂²u/∂t² = c² [f''(v) + g''(w)] = c² (∂²u/∂x²). Hence proved.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 5: substitute step 2 into step 4 to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: ∂²u/∂t² = c² (∂²u/∂x²) (Hence proved)
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Define intermediate variables v = x - ct, w = x + ct.
  • Show ∂v/∂t = -c and ∂w/∂t = +c explicitly.
  • Equate LHS and RHS to complete proof.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Missing the minus sign on (-c)(-c).
✓ How to Avoid: Careful with double negative: -c · (-c) = +c².
⚠️ Common Mistake: Omitting the prime notation f'(v).
✓ How to Avoid: Remember to use chain rule: df/dt = f'(v) · dv/dt.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 24: If u = f(x + iy) + g(x - iy), prove that ∂²u/∂x² + ∂²u/∂y² = 0 (Laplace equation).
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 1
QUESTION 25: Euler's Theorem for Three Variables with Algebraic-Logarithmic Form
Exam-Level
A. Full Question:
If u = log[(x⁴ + y⁴ + z⁴) / (x + y + z)], prove that x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y) + z(∂u/∂z) = 3.
B. Meaning of the Question

Apply Euler's theorem for three independent variables to e^u, find its degree of homogeneity, and prove the sum equals the degree 3.

C. Basic Concept

For three variables, Euler's Theorem states: x u_x + y u_y + z u_z = n u. For logarithmic functions, let f(u) = e^u, then x u_x + y u_y + z u_z = n.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: If e^u is homogeneous of degree n, then x u_x + y u_y + z u_z = n.

Explanation & Conditions: Since d(e^u)/dx = e^u u_x, the e^u cancels out from both sides, leaving simply n.

E. Given Information

u = log[(x⁴ + y⁴ + z⁴) / (x + y + z)].

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Eliminate logarithm by exponentiating both sides
e^u = (x⁴ + y⁴ + z⁴) / (x + y + z) = F(x, y, z).
💡 Why this step is performed: Apply elementary row operations to introduce zeros below the pivot element and reduce the matrix toward echelon form.
Step 2: Determine degree of homogeneity of F
F(tx, ty, tz) = [(tx)⁴ + (ty)⁴ + (tz)⁴] / [tx + ty + tz]
= [t⁴(x⁴ + y⁴ + z⁴)] / [t(x + y + z)] = t⁴⁻¹ · F = t³ F.
Hence, F = e^u is a homogeneous function of degree n = 3 in x, y, z.
💡 Why this step is performed: Substitute tx and ty for x and y to factor out t^n and establish the degree of homogeneity n.
Step 3: Apply Euler's Theorem to F = e^u
x(∂F/∂x) + y(∂F/∂y) + z(∂F/∂z) = n · F = 3 e^u.
💡 Why this step is performed: Combine the partial derivatives into the linear combination x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y) and verify it equals n·u.
Step 4: Differentiate F = e^u with respect to x, y, z
∂F/∂x = e^u (∂u/∂x)
∂F/∂y = e^u (∂u/∂y)
∂F/∂z = e^u (∂u/∂z).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: differentiate f = e^u with respect to x, y, z to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 5: Substitute and divide by e^u
x [e^u (∂u/∂x)] + y [e^u (∂u/∂y)] + z [e^u (∂u/∂z)] = 3 e^u
e^u [ x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y) + z(∂u/∂z) ] = 3 e^u
x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y) + z(∂u/∂z) = 3. Hence proved.
💡 Why this step is performed: Compute the sum of partial derivatives of the component functions along their respective coordinate axes.
✓ G. Final Answer: x(∂u/∂x) + y(∂u/∂y) + z(∂u/∂z) = 3 (Hence proved)
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Write F = e^u clearly.
  • Show F(tx, ty, tz) = t³ F to establish degree n = 3.
  • Factor out e^u and divide both sides.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Writing RHS as 3u.
✓ How to Avoid: For u = log(homogeneous), the answer is a CONSTANT n = 3, NOT 3u!
⚠️ Common Mistake: Attempting direct differentiation with quotient rule on log.
✓ How to Avoid: Direct differentiation takes 3 pages; Euler's theorem on e^u takes 5 lines.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 25: If u = log[(x³ + y³ + z³) / (x² + y² + z²)], prove that x u_x + y u_y + z u_z = 1.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
