VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 2
QUESTION 26: Gradient of a Scalar Field at a Point
Fundamental
A. Full Question:
If φ(x, y, z) = 3x²y - y³z², find grad φ (or ∇φ) at the point (1, -2, -1).
B. Meaning of the Question

Calculate the vector of spatial partial derivatives (gradient vector) of the scalar field φ at the specified point (1, -2, -1).

C. Basic Concept

The gradient ∇φ transforms a scalar field into a vector field. At any point, ∇φ points in the direction of greatest rate of increase of φ, and its magnitude gives the maximum rate of increase.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: ∇φ = (∂φ/∂x) î + (∂φ/∂y) ĵ + (∂φ/∂z) k̂.

Explanation & Conditions: î, ĵ, k̂ are unit basis vectors along x, y, z axes. Each component is a partial derivative.

E. Given Information

φ = 3x²y - y³z², point P(1, -2, -1).

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Compute ∂φ/∂x
∂φ/∂x = ∂/∂x(3x²y - y³z²) = 6xy - 0 = 6xy.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: compute ∂φ/∂x to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Compute ∂φ/∂y
∂φ/∂y = ∂/∂y(3x²y - y³z²) = 3x² - 3y²z².
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: compute ∂φ/∂y to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Compute ∂φ/∂z
∂φ/∂z = ∂/∂z(3x²y - y³z²) = 0 - 2y³z = -2y³z.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: compute ∂φ/∂z to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Form the gradient vector ∇φ
∇φ = (6xy) î + (3x² - 3y²z²) ĵ - (2y³z) k̂.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: form the gradient vector ∇φ to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 5: Evaluate at P(1, -2, -1)
• x-component: 6(1)(-2) = -12.
• y-component: 3(1)² - 3(-2)²(-1)² = 3(1) - 3(4)(1) = 3 - 12 = -9.
• z-component: -2(-2)³(-1) = -2(-8)(-1) = -16.
Therefore: ∇φ(1, -2, -1) = -12 î - 9 ĵ - 16 k̂.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 5: evaluate at p(1, -2, -1) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: ∇φ(1, -2, -1) = -12 î - 9 ĵ - 16 k̂
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • State formula for ∇φ first.
  • Calculate all 3 components separately.
  • Substitute coordinates carefully observing negative signs.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Sign error in (-2)³ = -8.
✓ How to Avoid: Cubing a negative number yields a negative number: (-2)³ = -8, then -2(-8)(-1) = -16.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Writing gradient as a scalar.
✓ How to Avoid: Gradient of a scalar field is always a VECTOR!
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 26: If φ = 2xz⁴ - x²y, find ∇φ at (2, -2, 1).
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 2
QUESTION 27: Divergence of a Vector Field
Fundamental
A. Full Question:
If F⃗ = (x²y) î + (y²z) ĵ + (z²x) k̂, find div F⃗ (or ∇ · F⃗) at the point (1, 2, 3).
B. Meaning of the Question

Compute the divergence of the vector field F⃗, which measures the net outward flux of vector field per unit volume from a point.

C. Basic Concept

Divergence is a scalar measure of a vector field's source or sink density. If div F⃗ > 0, the point acts as a source; if div F⃗ < 0, it acts as a sink; if div F⃗ = 0, the field is solenoidal (incompressible).

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: div F⃗ = ∇ · F⃗ = ∂F₁/∂x + ∂F₂/∂y + ∂F₃/∂z.

Explanation & Conditions: Dot product of the del operator ∇ with vector F⃗ = F₁î + F₂ĵ + F₃k̂ gives a SCALAR.

E. Given Information

F⃗ = (x²y) î + (y²z) ĵ + (z²x) k̂, point P(1, 2, 3).

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Identify components of F⃗
F₁ = x²y, F₂ = y²z, F₃ = z²x.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: identify components of f⃗ to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Differentiate each component with respect to its coordinate
∂F₁/∂x = ∂(x²y)/∂x = 2xy
∂F₂/∂y = ∂(y²z)/∂y = 2yz
∂F₃/∂z = ∂(z²x)/∂z = 2zx.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: differentiate each component with respect to its coordinate to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Add to get the scalar divergence
div F⃗ = ∇ · F⃗ = 2xy + 2yz + 2zx = 2(xy + yz + zx).
💡 Why this step is performed: Compute the sum of partial derivatives of the component functions along their respective coordinate axes.
Step 4: Evaluate at point (1, 2, 3)
div F⃗(1, 2, 3) = 2[(1)(2) + (2)(3) + (3)(1)] = 2[2 + 6 + 3] = 2(11) = 22.
💡 Why this step is performed: Substitute the specific point coordinates into the gradient vector components.
✓ G. Final Answer: div F⃗(1, 2, 3) = 22
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • State formula div F⃗ = ∂F₁/∂x + ∂F₂/∂y + ∂F₃/∂z.
  • Compute each term separately.
  • Add terms and substitute point values.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Including î, ĵ, k̂ in the divergence answer.
✓ How to Avoid: Divergence is a SCALAR product (dot product), so î, ĵ, k̂ MUST NOT appear!
⚠️ Common Mistake: Differentiating F₁ with respect to y instead of x.
✓ How to Avoid: F₁ is with respect to x, F₂ with y, F₃ with z.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 27: If F⃗ = (x³ + y³) î + (y³ + z³) ĵ + (z³ + x³) k̂, find div F⃗ at (1, 1, 1).
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 2
QUESTION 28: Curl of a Vector Field
Fundamental
A. Full Question:
If F⃗ = (xy²) î + (2x²yz) ĵ - (3yz²) k̂, find curl F⃗ (or ∇ × F⃗) at the point (1, -1, 1).
B. Meaning of the Question

Compute the curl of the vector field F⃗, which measures the infinitesimal rotation or circulation of the vector field around a point.

C. Basic Concept

Curl measures the vorticity or rotational tendency of a fluid flow. If curl F⃗ = 0⃗ everywhere, the field has no vortices and is called irrotational (conservative).

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: curl F⃗ = ∇ × F⃗ = | î ĵ k̂ |
| ∂/∂x ∂/∂y ∂/∂z |
| F₁ F₂ F₃ |.

Explanation & Conditions: = î(∂F₃/∂y - ∂F₂/∂z) - ĵ(∂F₃/∂x - ∂F₁/∂z) + k̂(∂F₂/∂x - ∂F₁/∂y).

E. Given Information

F⃗ = (xy²) î + (2x²yz) ĵ - (3yz²) k̂, point P(1, -1, 1).

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Set up the 3×3 curl determinant
∇ × F⃗ = | î ĵ k̂ |
| ∂/∂x ∂/∂y ∂/∂z |
| xy² 2x²yz -3yz² |.
💡 Why this step is performed: Set up the 3×3 curl determinant with unit vectors in row 1 and del operators in row 2.
Step 2: Expand î component
î [ ∂(-3yz²)/∂y - ∂(2x²yz)/∂z ] = î [ -3z² - 2x²y ].
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: expand î component to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Expand ĵ component (with negative sign)
-ĵ [ ∂(-3yz²)/∂x - ∂(xy²)/∂z ] = -ĵ [ 0 - 0 ] = 0 ĵ.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: expand ĵ component (with negative sign) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Expand k̂ component
k̂ [ ∂(2x²yz)/∂x - ∂(xy²)/∂y ] = k̂ [ 4xyz - 2xy ].
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: expand k̂ component to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 5: Combine into vector expression and evaluate at (1, -1, 1)
∇ × F⃗ = (-3z² - 2x²y) î + (4xyz - 2xy) k̂.
At (1, -1, 1):
• î-comp: -3(1)² - 2(1)²(-1) = -3 + 2 = -1.
• ĵ-comp: 0.
• k̂-comp: 4(1)(-1)(1) - 2(1)(-1) = -4 + 2 = -2.
Thus, curl F⃗ = -î - 2k̂.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 5: combine into vector expression and evaluate at (1, -1, 1) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: curl F⃗(1, -1, 1) = -î - 2k̂
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Write out the 3×3 determinant with del operators in row 2.
  • Show the cross-derivative pairs for each component.
  • Evaluate at the point.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Sign error on ĵ component.
✓ How to Avoid: Remember the cofactor of ĵ has a minus sign: -ĵ(∂F₃/∂x - ∂F₁/∂z).
⚠️ Common Mistake: Treating curl as a scalar.
✓ How to Avoid: Curl of a vector is a VECTOR!
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 28: If F⃗ = (x² - y²) î + 2xy ĵ + (y² - z²) k̂, find curl F⃗ at (1, 2, 3).
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).

QUESTION 29: Unit Normal Vector to a Given Surface
Fundamental
A. Full Question:
Find the unit normal vector n̂ to the surface x² + y² + 2z² = 26 at the point (2, 2, 3).
B. Meaning of the Question

Find a vector of length 1 that is perpendicular to the tangent plane of the given ellipsoid surface at (2, 2, 3).

C. Basic Concept

For any level surface φ(x, y, z) = C, the gradient vector ∇φ is perpendicular (normal) to the tangent plane at every point. Dividing ∇φ by its magnitude gives the unit normal vector n̂.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: n̂ = ∇φ / |∇φ|.

Explanation & Conditions: ∇φ is the normal vector, |∇φ| is its Euclidean length √(Ax² + Ay² + Az²).

E. Given Information

Surface φ(x, y, z) = x² + y² + 2z² - 26 = 0, point P(2, 2, 3).

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Compute the gradient vector ∇φ
∂φ/∂x = 2x, ∂φ/∂y = 2y, ∂φ/∂z = 4z.
∇φ = (2x) î + (2y) ĵ + (4z) k̂.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: compute the gradient vector ∇φ to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Evaluate ∇φ at point (2, 2, 3)
∇φ(2, 2, 3) = 2(2) î + 2(2) ĵ + 4(3) k̂ = 4 î + 4 ĵ + 12 k̂.
Factor out 4: ∇φ = 4(î + ĵ + 3k̂).
💡 Why this step is performed: Substitute the specific point coordinates into the gradient vector components.
Step 3: Compute the magnitude |∇φ|
|∇φ| = √[ 4² + 4² + 12² ] = √[ 16 + 16 + 144 ] = √176 = √(16 · 11) = 4√11.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: compute the magnitude |∇φ| to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Divide vector by its magnitude to obtain n̂
n̂ = (4 î + 4 ĵ + 12 k̂) / (4√11) = (î + ĵ + 3k̂) / √11.
💡 Why this step is performed: Compute the sum of partial derivatives of the component functions along their respective coordinate axes.
✓ G. Final Answer: n̂ = (î + ĵ + 3k̂) / √11 = (1/√11) î + (1/√11) ĵ + (3/√11) k̂
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Define φ(x, y, z) = x² + y² + 2z².
  • State n̂ = ∇φ / |∇φ|.
  • Cancel the common factor 4 before writing final answer.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Leaving answer as ∇φ without normalizing.
✓ How to Avoid: The question asks for UNIT normal, so dividing by magnitude is compulsory!
⚠️ Common Mistake: Arithmetic error in 4(3) = 12.
✓ How to Avoid: Check 2(2z) = 4z, so at z=3, 4(3)=12, not 6.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 29: Find the unit normal vector to the sphere x² + y² + z² = 9 at (1, 2, 2).
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).

QUESTION 30: Directional Derivative Along a Given Vector
Fundamental
A. Full Question:
Find the directional derivative of φ(x, y, z) = 2xy + z² at the point (1, -1, 3) in the direction of the vector a⃗ = î + 2ĵ + 2k̂.
B. Meaning of the Question

Compute the rate of change of the scalar field φ at (1, -1, 3) along the specific direction defined by vector a⃗.

C. Basic Concept

The directional derivative is the projection of the gradient ∇φ onto the unit direction vector â: D_a φ = ∇φ · â. It gives the rate of change per unit distance along that direction.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: D_u φ = ∇φ · â = ∇φ · (a⃗ / |a⃗|).

Explanation & Conditions: â must be a UNIT vector of length 1.

E. Given Information

φ = 2xy + z², point P(1, -1, 3), direction vector a⃗ = î + 2ĵ + 2k̂.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Compute gradient ∇φ
∂φ/∂x = 2y, ∂φ/∂y = 2x, ∂φ/∂z = 2z.
∇φ = (2y) î + (2x) ĵ + (2z) k̂.
💡 Why this step is performed: Calculate the spatial partial derivatives along each coordinate axis to form the gradient vector ∇φ.
Step 2: Evaluate ∇φ at point (1, -1, 3)
∇φ(1, -1, 3) = 2(-1) î + 2(1) ĵ + 2(3) k̂ = -2 î + 2 ĵ + 6 k̂.
💡 Why this step is performed: Substitute the specific point coordinates into the gradient vector components.
Step 3: Find the unit vector â in the direction of a⃗
|a⃗| = √(1² + 2² + 2²) = √(1 + 4 + 4) = √9 = 3.
â = a⃗ / |a⃗| = (î + 2ĵ + 2k̂) / 3.
💡 Why this step is performed: Divide the direction vector by its Euclidean length to obtain a normalized unit vector of magnitude 1.
Step 4: Take the dot product ∇φ · â
D_a φ = (-2 î + 2 ĵ + 6 k̂) · [ (î + 2ĵ + 2k̂) / 3 ]
= [ (-2)(1) + (2)(2) + (6)(2) ] / 3
= [ -2 + 4 + 12 ] / 3 = 14 / 3.
💡 Why this step is performed: Take the scalar dot product between the gradient vector and the unit direction vector.
✓ G. Final Answer: Directional Derivative = 14 / 3
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Compute ∇φ at the point clearly.
  • Show unit vector â = a⃗ / |a⃗| with |a⃗| = 3.
  • Show the dot product arithmetic explicitly.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Taking dot product with a⃗ instead of unit vector â.
✓ How to Avoid: You MUST divide by |a⃗| = 3! Taking dot product with non-unit vector is completely wrong.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Sign error in (-2)(1) = -2.
✓ How to Avoid: Keep track of negative signs carefully.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 30: Find the directional derivative of φ = x²y + yz² at (2, -1, 1) in direction 2î - ĵ - 2k̂.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).

QUESTION 31: Maximum Directional Derivative and Its Direction
Intermediate
A. Full Question:
Find the maximum directional derivative of φ(x, y, z) = x²y²z⁴ at the point (1, 1, -1) and state the direction in which it occurs.
B. Meaning of the Question

Determine the direction of fastest increase of φ at (1, 1, -1) and the maximum rate of change.

C. Basic Concept

The directional derivative ∇φ · û = |∇φ| cos θ attains its maximum value when cos θ = 1 (θ = 0), which occurs precisely in the direction of ∇φ itself, with maximum value equal to |∇φ|.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: Maximum value = |∇φ|; Direction of maximum = ∇φ (or unit vector ∇φ/|∇φ|).

Explanation & Conditions: The gradient vector always points in the direction of steepest ascent.

E. Given Information

φ = x²y²z⁴, point P(1, 1, -1).

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Compute partial derivatives
∂φ/∂x = 2xy²z⁴, ∂φ/∂y = 2x²yz⁴, ∂φ/∂z = 4x²y²z³.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: compute partial derivatives to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Evaluate ∇φ at P(1, 1, -1)
∂φ/∂x = 2(1)(1)²(-1)⁴ = 2(1) = 2.
∂φ/∂y = 2(1)²(1)(-1)⁴ = 2(1) = 2.
∂φ/∂z = 4(1)²(1)²(-1)³ = 4(-1) = -4.
∇φ = 2 î + 2 ĵ - 4 k̂.
💡 Why this step is performed: Substitute the specific point coordinates into the gradient vector components.
Step 3: State the direction of maximum directional derivative
The maximum directional derivative occurs along the vector ∇φ = 2 î + 2 ĵ - 4 k̂ (or unit direction (î + ĵ - 2k̂)/√6).
💡 Why this step is performed: Take the scalar dot product between the gradient vector and the unit direction vector.
Step 4: Compute the magnitude |∇φ|
|∇φ| = √[ 2² + 2² + (-4)² ] = √[ 4 + 4 + 16 ] = √24 = √(4 · 6) = 2√6.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: compute the magnitude |∇φ| to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: Maximum Directional Derivative = 2√6; Direction: along 2 î + 2 ĵ - 4 k̂ (or unit vector (î + ĵ - 2k̂)/√6)
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • State property: Max directional derivative = |∇φ| in direction of ∇φ.
  • Show ∇φ calculation.
  • Simplify √24 into 2√6.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Sign error on (-1)³.
✓ How to Avoid: (-1)³ = -1, so z-component is -4, not +4.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Forgetting to state the direction.
✓ How to Avoid: The question asks for both the maximum value AND the direction.
J. Similar Practice Question

(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).

QUESTION 32: Condition for a Solenoidal Vector Field
Intermediate
A. Full Question:
Find the constant 'a' such that the vector field F⃗ = (x + 3y) î + (y - 2z) ĵ + (x + az) k̂ is solenoidal.
B. Meaning of the Question

Determine the value of parameter 'a' for which the divergence of vector field F⃗ is identically zero.

C. Basic Concept

A vector field is solenoidal if its divergence is zero: ∇ · F⃗ = 0. This physically represents an incompressible fluid with no sources or sinks.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: div F⃗ = ∂F₁/∂x + ∂F₂/∂y + ∂F₃/∂z = 0.

Explanation & Conditions: Sum of coordinate derivatives must equate to zero.

E. Given Information

F⃗ = (x + 3y) î + (y - 2z) ĵ + (x + az) k̂.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Identify components of F⃗
F₁ = x + 3y, F₂ = y - 2z, F₃ = x + az.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: identify components of f⃗ to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Differentiate each component
∂F₁/∂x = ∂(x + 3y)/∂x = 1
∂F₂/∂y = ∂(y - 2z)/∂y = 1
∂F₃/∂z = ∂(x + az)/∂z = a.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: differentiate each component to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Apply the solenoidal condition div F⃗ = 0
∇ · F⃗ = ∂F₁/∂x + ∂F₂/∂y + ∂F₃/∂z = 1 + 1 + a = 2 + a = 0.
💡 Why this step is performed: Compute the sum of partial derivatives of the component functions along their respective coordinate axes.
Step 4: Solve for 'a'
2 + a = 0 ⇒ a = -2.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: solve for 'a' to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: a = -2
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • State condition: For F⃗ to be solenoidal, div F⃗ = 0.
  • Show term-by-term divergence: 1 + 1 + a = 0.
  • Conclude a = -2 in a box.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Confusing solenoidal with irrotational.
✓ How to Avoid: Solenoidal means div F⃗ = 0 (dot product). Irrotational means curl F⃗ = 0 (cross product).
⚠️ Common Mistake: Derivative of az with respect to z written as 0.
✓ How to Avoid: d(az)/dz = a.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 32: Find constant 'k' such that F⃗ = (2x + y) î + (ky - z) ĵ + (3x - 4z) k̂ is solenoidal.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 2
QUESTION 33: Irrotational Vector Field and Finding Constants
Intermediate
A. Full Question:
Find constants a, b, c so that the vector field F⃗ = (x + 2y + az) î + (bx - 3y - z) ĵ + (4x + cy + 2z) k̂ is irrotational.
B. Meaning of the Question

Determine values of a, b, c such that curl F⃗ = 0⃗ everywhere.

C. Basic Concept

A vector field is irrotational if its curl is identically zero: ∇ × F⃗ = 0⃗. Equating each of the three components of curl to zero produces three algebraic equations for a, b, c.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: curl F⃗ = î(∂F₃/∂y - ∂F₂/∂z) + ĵ(∂F₁/∂z - ∂F₃/∂x) + k̂(∂F₂/∂x - ∂F₁/∂y) = 0 î + 0 ĵ + 0 k̂.

Explanation & Conditions: Each bracketed difference must equal zero.

E. Given Information

F₁ = x + 2y + az, F₂ = bx - 3y - z, F₃ = 4x + cy + 2z.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Set up curl components
• î-component: ∂F₃/∂y - ∂F₂/∂z = c - (-1) = c + 1 = 0.
• ĵ-component: ∂F₁/∂z - ∂F₃/∂x = a - 4 = 0.
• k̂-component: ∂F₂/∂x - ∂F₁/∂y = b - 2 = 0.
💡 Why this step is performed: Set up the 3×3 curl determinant with unit vectors in row 1 and del operators in row 2.
Step 2: Solve each equation
From î-component: c + 1 = 0 ⇒ c = -1.
From ĵ-component: a - 4 = 0 ⇒ a = 4.
From k̂-component: b - 2 = 0 ⇒ b = 2.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: solve each equation to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: a = 4, b = 2, c = -1
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Write out curl determinant clearly.
  • Equate each vector component to 0 separately.
  • List a = 4, b = 2, c = -1.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Sign error in c - (-1).
✓ How to Avoid: ∂F₂/∂z = -1, so c - (-1) = c + 1 = 0 ⇒ c = -1.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Swapping a and b.
✓ How to Avoid: Double check which component corresponds to which derivative.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 33: Find a, b, c such that F⃗ = (axy + z³) î + (3x² - cz) ĵ + (3xz² - y) k̂ is irrotational.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 2
QUESTION 34: Finding Scalar Potential Function φ for an Irrotational Field
Intermediate
A. Full Question:
Show that F⃗ = (y² cos x + z³) î + (2y sin x - 4) ĵ + (3xz²) k̂ is conservative and find its scalar potential φ such that F⃗ = ∇φ.
B. Meaning of the Question

Prove curl F⃗ = 0⃗ (conservative), then integrate the three component differential equations to reconstruct the scalar potential φ.

C. Basic Concept

By Helmholtz decomposition, any irrotational vector field can be expressed as the gradient of a scalar potential: F⃗ = ∇φ. Then dφ = F₁ dx + F₂ dy + F₃ dz is an exact differential.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: ∂φ/∂x = F₁, ∂φ/∂y = F₂, ∂φ/∂z = F₃; φ = ∫ F₁ dx (treating y, z const) + ∫ (terms in F₂ free of x) dy + ∫ (terms in F₃ free of x, y) dz.

Explanation & Conditions: Standard exact differential integration technique avoids duplicate terms.

E. Given Information

F₁ = y² cos x + z³, F₂ = 2y sin x - 4, F₃ = 3xz².

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Verify curl F⃗ = 0⃗
• î-comp: ∂(3xz²)/∂y - ∂(2y sin x - 4)/∂z = 0 - 0 = 0.
• ĵ-comp: ∂(y² cos x + z³)/∂z - ∂(3xz²)/∂x = 3z² - 3z² = 0.
• k̂-comp: ∂(2y sin x - 4)/∂x - ∂(y² cos x + z³)/∂y = 2y cos x - 2y cos x = 0.
Since curl F⃗ = 0⃗, F⃗ is conservative.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: verify curl f⃗ = 0⃗ to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Set up potential equations
∂φ/∂x = y² cos x + z³ --- (1)
∂φ/∂y = 2y sin x - 4 --- (2)
∂φ/∂z = 3xz² --- (3).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: set up potential equations to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Integrate equation (1) with respect to x
φ = ∫ (y² cos x + z³) dx = y² sin x + x z³ + f(y, z).
💡 Why this step is performed: Integrate the exact differential equations dφ = F₁ dx + F₂ dy + F₃ dz systematically to reconstruct the scalar potential.
Step 4: Differentiate with respect to y and compare with (2)
∂φ/∂y = 2y sin x + ∂f/∂y = 2y sin x - 4 ⇒ ∂f/∂y = -4.
Integrate: f(y, z) = -4y + g(z).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: differentiate with respect to y and compare with (2) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 5: Differentiate with respect to z and compare with (3)
∂φ/∂z = 3xz² + g'(z) = 3xz² ⇒ g'(z) = 0 ⇒ g(z) = C.
Therefore: φ(x, y, z) = y² sin x + x z³ - 4y + C.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 5: differentiate with respect to z and compare with (3) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: φ(x, y, z) = y² sin x + x z³ - 4y + C
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Show curl F⃗ = 0⃗ first to justify existence of φ.
  • Integrate step-by-step matching arbitrary functions f(y, z) and g(z).
  • Include arbitrary constant C.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Adding duplicate terms (e.g., adding xz³ twice).
✓ How to Avoid: xz³ appears in both x and z integrals; it must only be written ONCE in φ!
⚠️ Common Mistake: Forgetting arbitrary constant C.
✓ How to Avoid: Potential function is determined up to an additive constant C.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 34: Show F⃗ = (2xy + z³) î + x² ĵ + 3xz² k̂ is conservative and find its scalar potential.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 2
QUESTION 35: Angle Between Two Surfaces at Point of Intersection
Intermediate
A. Full Question:
Find the angle between the surfaces x² + y² + z² = 9 and z = x² + y² - 3 at the point (2, -1, 2).
B. Meaning of the Question

Compute the angle between the normal vectors of the two surfaces at their intersection point (2, -1, 2).

C. Basic Concept

The angle θ between two surfaces at a common point is defined as the angle between their normal vectors (gradients) at that point: cos θ = (∇φ₁ · ∇φ₂) / (|∇φ₁| |∇φ₂|).

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: cos θ = (n⃗₁ · n⃗₂) / (|n⃗₁| |n⃗₂|), where n⃗₁ = ∇φ₁ and n⃗₂ = ∇φ₂.

Explanation & Conditions: Standard dot product angle formula applied to gradient normals.

E. Given Information

φ₁ = x² + y² + z² - 9 = 0, φ₂ = x² + y² - z - 3 = 0, point P(2, -1, 2).

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Compute normal vector n⃗₁ = ∇φ₁ at P(2, -1, 2)
∇φ₁ = 2x î + 2y ĵ + 2z k̂.
At (2, -1, 2): n⃗₁ = 2(2) î + 2(-1) ĵ + 2(2) k̂ = 4 î - 2 ĵ + 4 k̂.
|n⃗₁| = √[ 4² + (-2)² + 4² ] = √[ 16 + 4 + 16 ] = √36 = 6.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: compute normal vector n⃗₁ = ∇φ₁ at p(2, -1, 2) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Compute normal vector n⃗₂ = ∇φ₂ at P(2, -1, 2)
∇φ₂ = 2x î + 2y ĵ - k̂.
At (2, -1, 2): n⃗₂ = 2(2) î + 2(-1) ĵ - k̂ = 4 î - 2 ĵ - k̂.
|n⃗₂| = √[ 4² + (-2)² + (-1)² ] = √[ 16 + 4 + 1 ] = √21.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: compute normal vector n⃗₂ = ∇φ₂ at p(2, -1, 2) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Compute dot product n⃗₁ · n⃗₂
n⃗₁ · n⃗₂ = (4)(4) + (-2)(-2) + (4)(-1) = 16 + 4 - 4 = 16.
💡 Why this step is performed: Take the scalar dot product between the gradient vector and the unit direction vector.
Step 4: Calculate cos θ and θ
cos θ = (n⃗₁ · n⃗₂) / (|n⃗₁| |n⃗₂|) = 16 / (6 · √21) = 8 / (3√21).
θ = cos⁻¹ [ 8 / (3√21) ].
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: calculate cos θ and θ to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: θ = cos⁻¹ [ 8 / (3√21) ] ≈ 54.4°
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Find ∇φ₁ and ∇φ₂ at (2, -1, 2).
  • Compute lengths |n⃗₁| and |n⃗₂| clearly.
  • Apply dot product formula and simplify fraction 16/6 = 8/3.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Taking ∂φ₂/∂z as +1 instead of -1.
✓ How to Avoid: From z = x² + y² - 3, φ₂ = x² + y² - z - 3, so ∂φ₂/∂z = -1.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Forgetting to take inverse cosine.
✓ How to Avoid: State θ = cos⁻¹(...) clearly.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 35: Find angle between surfaces x² + y² + z² = 29 and x² + y² + z² + 4x - 6y - 8z - 47 = 0 at (4, -3, 2).
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 2
QUESTION 36: Vector Identity: div(curl F⃗) = 0
Intermediate
A. Full Question:
Prove that for any twice continuously differentiable vector field F⃗, div(curl F⃗) = 0 (or ∇ · (∇ × F⃗) = 0).
B. Meaning of the Question

Prove the fundamental vector identity stating that the divergence of any curl field is identically zero.

C. Basic Concept

The boundary of a boundary is zero. Physically, curl represents pure circulating flow with closed field lines; closed loops have no starting points (sources) or ending points (sinks), so their net divergence is always zero.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: ∇ · (∇ × F⃗) = 0.

Explanation & Conditions: Follows from equality of mixed partial derivatives ∂²/∂x∂y = ∂²/∂y∂x.

E. Given Information

F⃗ = F₁ î + F₂ ĵ + F₃ k̂.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Write curl F⃗ in component form
curl F⃗ = (∂F₃/∂y - ∂F₂/∂z) î + (∂F₁/∂z - ∂F₃/∂x) ĵ + (∂F₂/∂x - ∂F₁/∂y) k̂.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: write curl f⃗ in component form to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Take divergence (dot with del operator)
div(curl F⃗) = ∂/∂x(∂F₃/∂y - ∂F₂/∂z) + ∂/∂y(∂F₁/∂z - ∂F₃/∂x) + ∂/∂z(∂F₂/∂x - ∂F₁/∂y).
💡 Why this step is performed: Compute the sum of partial derivatives of the component functions along their respective coordinate axes.
Step 3: Expand the partial derivatives
= (∂²F₃/∂x∂y - ∂²F₂/∂x∂z) + (∂²F₁/∂y∂z - ∂²F₃/∂y∂x) + (∂²F₂/∂z∂x - ∂²F₁/∂z∂y).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: expand the partial derivatives to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Group pairs using Clairaut's theorem
= (∂²F₃/∂x∂y - ∂²F₃/∂y∂x) + (∂²F₂/∂z∂x - ∂²F₂/∂x∂z) + (∂²F₁/∂y∂z - ∂²F₁/∂z∂y)
= 0 + 0 + 0 = 0. Hence proved.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: group pairs using clairaut's theorem to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: div(curl F⃗) = ∇ · (∇ × F⃗) = 0 (Hence proved)
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Write components of curl F⃗.
  • Apply divergence operator.
  • Group identical mixed partials with opposite signs to show cancellation.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Skipping Step 3 and jumping directly to 0.
✓ How to Avoid: You must write out all 6 second-order mixed partial derivatives to get full marks!
⚠️ Common Mistake: Confusing with curl(grad φ) = 0.
✓ How to Avoid: Both are zero, but be sure to write the correct one requested.
J. Similar Practice Question

(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 2
QUESTION 37: Properties of Position Vector r⃗: Divergence and Curl
Intermediate
A. Full Question:
If r⃗ = x î + y ĵ + z k̂ and r = |r⃗| = √(x² + y² + z²), find (i) div r⃗ and (ii) curl r⃗.
B. Meaning of the Question

Evaluate the divergence and curl of the standard 3D position vector field r⃗.

C. Basic Concept

The position vector represents uniform radial expansion from the origin. It has constant divergence 3 and zero curl everywhere (purely irrotational).

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: div r⃗ = ∂x/∂x + ∂y/∂y + ∂z/∂z; curl r⃗ = ∇ × r⃗.

Explanation & Conditions: Basic operations on fundamental coordinate vector.

E. Given Information

r⃗ = x î + y ĵ + z k̂.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Compute div r⃗
div r⃗ = ∇ · r⃗ = ∂(x)/∂x + ∂(y)/∂y + ∂(z)/∂z = 1 + 1 + 1 = 3.
💡 Why this step is performed: Compute the sum of partial derivatives of the component functions along their respective coordinate axes.
Step 2: Set up curl r⃗ determinant
curl r⃗ = | î ĵ k̂ |
| ∂/∂x ∂/∂y ∂/∂z |
| x y z |.
💡 Why this step is performed: Set up the 3×3 curl determinant with unit vectors in row 1 and del operators in row 2.
Step 3: Expand the determinant
= î [ ∂z/∂y - ∂y/∂z ] - ĵ [ ∂z/∂x - ∂x/∂z ] + k̂ [ ∂y/∂x - ∂x/∂y ]
= î [ 0 - 0 ] - ĵ [ 0 - 0 ] + k̂ [ 0 - 0 ] = 0 î + 0 ĵ + 0 k̂ = 0⃗.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: expand the determinant to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: (i) div r⃗ = 3; (ii) curl r⃗ = 0⃗
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Show div r⃗ = 1 + 1 + 1 = 3.
  • Show curl r⃗ determinant with zeros.
  • Write zero vector 0⃗ with arrow or bold notation.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Writing curl r⃗ = 0 (scalar) instead of 0⃗ (zero vector).
✓ How to Avoid: Curl produces a VECTOR, so write 0⃗ or 0î + 0ĵ + 0k̂.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Writing div r⃗ = 1.
✓ How to Avoid: Each axis contributes 1: 1 + 1 + 1 = 3.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 37: Find div(r⃗ / r³) for r ≠ 0.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 2
QUESTION 38: Gradient of Radial Power Function: ∇(rⁿ)
Intermediate
A. Full Question:
Prove that ∇(rⁿ) = n rⁿ⁻² r⃗.
B. Meaning of the Question

Find the gradient of the power of magnitude of position vector rⁿ.

C. Basic Concept

Since r = √(x² + y² + z²), ∂r/∂x = x/r. Applying chain rule to rⁿ gives (n rⁿ⁻¹)(x/r) = n rⁿ⁻² x. Combining components gives n rⁿ⁻² r⃗.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: ∇(rⁿ) = Σ î [∂(rⁿ)/∂x] = n rⁿ⁻² r⃗.

Explanation & Conditions: ∂r/∂x = x/r, ∂r/∂y = y/r, ∂r/∂z = z/r.

E. Given Information

r² = x² + y² + z², φ = rⁿ.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Compute ∂r/∂x
Differentiating r² = x² + y² + z² partially with respect to x:
2r (∂r/∂x) = 2x ⇒ ∂r/∂x = x/r.
Similarly, ∂r/∂y = y/r and ∂r/∂z = z/r.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: compute ∂r/∂x to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Differentiate rⁿ with respect to x using chain rule
∂(rⁿ)/∂x = n rⁿ⁻¹ · (∂r/∂x) = n rⁿ⁻¹ · (x/r) = n rⁿ⁻² x.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: differentiate rⁿ with respect to x using chain rule to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Construct gradient vector ∇(rⁿ)
∇(rⁿ) = [∂(rⁿ)/∂x] î + [∂(rⁿ)/∂y] ĵ + [∂(rⁿ)/∂z] k̂
= (n rⁿ⁻² x) î + (n rⁿ⁻² y) ĵ + (n rⁿ⁻² z) k̂.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: construct gradient vector ∇(rⁿ) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Factor out common scalar n rⁿ⁻²
= n rⁿ⁻² [ x î + y ĵ + z k̂ ] = n rⁿ⁻² r⃗. Hence proved.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: factor out common scalar n rⁿ⁻² to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: ∇(rⁿ) = n rⁿ⁻² r⃗ (Hence proved)
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Establish ∂r/∂x = x/r clearly.
  • Show chain rule: d(rⁿ)/dx = n rⁿ⁻¹ (x/r) = n rⁿ⁻² x.
  • Factor out n rⁿ⁻² and replace with r⃗.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Writing n rⁿ⁻¹ r⃗ instead of n rⁿ⁻² r⃗.
✓ How to Avoid: Dividing x by r reduces the exponent of r by another 1, making it n - 2!
⚠️ Common Mistake: Forgetting that r depends on x, y, z.
✓ How to Avoid: r is not a constant; r = √(x²+y²+z²).
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 38: Find ∇(1/r) using the formula ∇(rⁿ) with n = -1.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 2
QUESTION 39: Laplacian of Radial Power Function: ∇²(rⁿ)
Intermediate
A. Full Question:
Prove that ∇²(rⁿ) = n(n + 1) rⁿ⁻² and deduce the value of n for which rⁿ is harmonic (∇²(rⁿ) = 0, r ≠ 0).
B. Meaning of the Question

Evaluate the Laplacian div(grad rⁿ) and find which power makes it satisfy Laplace's equation.

C. Basic Concept

Laplacian is ∇²φ = ∇ · ∇φ. A function is harmonic if ∇²φ = 0.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: ∇²(rⁿ) = div [ ∇(rⁿ) ] = ∇ · [ n rⁿ⁻² r⃗ ].

Explanation & Conditions: Vector identity: ∇ · (φ A⃗) = φ(∇ · A⃗) + ∇φ · A⃗.

E. Given Information

From Q38: ∇(rⁿ) = n rⁿ⁻² r⃗.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Express Laplacian as divergence of gradient
∇²(rⁿ) = ∇ · [ n rⁿ⁻² r⃗ ] = n [ ∇ · (rⁿ⁻² r⃗) ].
💡 Why this step is performed: Compute the sum of partial derivatives of the component functions along their respective coordinate axes.
Step 2: Apply product rule ∇ · (φ A⃗) = φ(∇ · A⃗) + (∇φ) · A⃗
Here φ = rⁿ⁻² and A⃗ = r⃗.
∇ · (rⁿ⁻² r⃗) = rⁿ⁻² (∇ · r⃗) + ∇(rⁿ⁻²) · r⃗.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: apply product rule ∇ · (φ a⃗) = φ(∇ · a⃗) + (∇φ) · a⃗ to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Substitute known results
From Q37: ∇ · r⃗ = 3.
From Q38: ∇(rⁿ⁻²) = (n - 2) rⁿ⁻⁴ r⃗.
Therefore: ∇(rⁿ⁻²) · r⃗ = (n - 2) rⁿ⁻⁴ (r⃗ · r⃗) = (n - 2) rⁿ⁻⁴ (r²) = (n - 2) rⁿ⁻².
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: substitute known results to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Combine the terms
∇ · (rⁿ⁻² r⃗) = rⁿ⁻² (3) + (n - 2) rⁿ⁻² = [ 3 + n - 2 ] rⁿ⁻² = (n + 1) rⁿ⁻².
Multiply by n:
∇²(rⁿ) = n (n + 1) rⁿ⁻². Hence proved.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: combine the terms to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 5: Find condition for harmonicity
For rⁿ to be harmonic: ∇²(rⁿ) = 0
n(n + 1) rⁿ⁻² = 0 ⇒ n(n + 1) = 0 (since r ≠ 0)
⇒ n = 0 or n = -1.
For n = -1: φ = 1/r (Newtonian/Coulomb potential).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 5: find condition for harmonicity to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: ∇²(rⁿ) = n(n + 1) rⁿ⁻²; Harmonic for n = 0 or n = -1 (i.e., 1/r is harmonic in 3D)
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Use ∇² = ∇ · ∇.
  • Use product rule ∇ · (u v⃗) = u div v⃗ + ∇u · v⃗.
  • Equate n(n+1)=0 to deduce harmonic powers n = 0, -1.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Writing ∇ · r⃗ = 1 instead of 3.
✓ How to Avoid: In 3D, ∇ · r⃗ = 3!
⚠️ Common Mistake: Missing r⃗ · r⃗ = r².
✓ How to Avoid: The dot product of a vector with itself is the square of its magnitude.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 39: Verify directly that φ = 1/r satisfies Laplace's equation ∂²φ/∂x² + ∂²φ/∂y² + ∂²φ/∂z² = 0.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 2
QUESTION 40: Scale Factors in Cylindrical Curvilinear Coordinates
Intermediate
A. Full Question:
For cylindrical coordinates (u, v, w) = (ρ, φ, z) where x = ρ cos φ, y = ρ sin φ, z = z, find the scale factors h₁, h₂, h₃ and show the system is orthogonal.
B. Meaning of the Question

Compute scale factors h_ρ, h_φ, h_z from the position vector r⃗ and verify that the coordinate basis vectors are mutually perpendicular.

C. Basic Concept

Curvilinear scale factor h_i = |∂r⃗/∂u_i|. The system is orthogonal if (∂r⃗/∂u_i) · (∂r⃗/∂u_j) = 0 for all i ≠ j.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: r⃗ = (ρ cos φ) î + (ρ sin φ) ĵ + z k̂; h_i = |∂r⃗/∂u_i|.

Explanation & Conditions: ds² = h₁² du₁² + h₂² du₂² + h₃² du₃².

E. Given Information

x = ρ cos φ, y = ρ sin φ, z = z.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Compute tangent vectors ∂r⃗/∂ρ, ∂r⃗/∂φ, ∂r⃗/∂z
• ∂r⃗/∂ρ = cos φ î + sin φ ĵ + 0 k̂
• ∂r⃗/∂φ = -ρ sin φ î + ρ cos φ ĵ + 0 k̂
• ∂r⃗/∂z = 0 î + 0 ĵ + 1 k̂.
💡 Why this step is performed: Compute the partial derivative of the position vector with respect to each curvilinear coordinate and evaluate its norm.
Step 2: Compute scale factors h₁, h₂, h₃
• h₁ = h_ρ = |∂r⃗/∂ρ| = √(cos² φ + sin² φ) = √1 = 1.
• h₂ = h_φ = |∂r⃗/∂φ| = √[ (-ρ sin φ)² + (ρ cos φ)² ] = √(ρ² sin² φ + ρ² cos² φ) = √ρ² = ρ.
• h₃ = h_z = |∂r⃗/∂z| = √(0 + 0 + 1²) = 1.
💡 Why this step is performed: Compute the partial derivative of the position vector with respect to each curvilinear coordinate and evaluate its norm.
Step 3: Verify orthogonality by dot products
• (∂r⃗/∂ρ) · (∂r⃗/∂φ) = (cos φ)(-ρ sin φ) + (sin φ)(ρ cos φ) + 0 = -ρ sin φ cos φ + ρ sin φ cos φ = 0.
• (∂r⃗/∂ρ) · (∂r⃗/∂z) = 0 + 0 + 0 = 0.
• (∂r⃗/∂φ) · (∂r⃗/∂z) = 0 + 0 + 0 = 0.
All mutual dot products are zero, so the cylindrical coordinate system is ORTHOGONAL.
💡 Why this step is performed: Take the scalar dot product between the gradient vector and the unit direction vector.
✓ G. Final Answer: Scale factors: h₁ = 1, h₂ = ρ, h₃ = 1; System is Orthogonal (Verified)
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Write position vector r⃗(ρ, φ, z).
  • Find partial derivatives ∂r⃗/∂ρ, ∂r⃗/∂φ, ∂r⃗/∂z.
  • Compute magnitudes for h and dot products for orthogonality.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Writing h₂ = 1 instead of ρ.
✓ How to Avoid: Remember ∂r⃗/∂φ has factor ρ, so its magnitude is ρ, NOT 1!
⚠️ Common Mistake: Leaving out the orthogonality verification.
✓ How to Avoid: Always show dot products equal 0.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 40: Find the scale factors h_r, h_θ, h_φ for spherical polar coordinates.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 2
QUESTION 41: Irrotational Field and Scalar Potential: Complete Examination Problem
Exam-Level
A. Full Question:
Show that F⃗ = (y² - z² + 3yz - 2x) î + (3xz + 2xy) ĵ + (3xy - 2xz + 2z) k̂ is irrotational and find its scalar potential function φ.
B. Meaning of the Question

Verify curl F⃗ = 0⃗ everywhere, then integrate to obtain the scalar field φ satisfying F⃗ = ∇φ.

C. Basic Concept

Standard 8-mark VTU exam problem. Requires full curl expansion and systematic integration of exact differential dφ = F₁ dx + F₂ dy + F₃ dz.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: curl F⃗ = 0⃗; φ = ∫ F₁ dx + ∫ (terms in F₂ without x) dy + ∫ (terms in F₃ without x, y) dz + C.

Explanation & Conditions: F₁ = ∂φ/∂x, F₂ = ∂φ/∂y, F₃ = ∂φ/∂z.

E. Given Information

F₁ = y² - z² + 3yz - 2x, F₂ = 3xz + 2xy, F₃ = 3xy - 2xz + 2z.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Compute curl F⃗
• î-comp: ∂F₃/∂y - ∂F₂/∂z = ∂(3xy - 2xz + 2z)/∂y - ∂(3xz + 2xy)/∂z = 3x - 3x = 0.
• ĵ-comp: ∂F₁/∂z - ∂F₃/∂x = ∂(y² - z² + 3yz - 2x)/∂z - ∂(3xy - 2xz + 2z)/∂x = (-2z + 3y) - (3y - 2z) = 0.
• k̂-comp: ∂F₂/∂x - ∂F₁/∂y = ∂(3xz + 2xy)/∂x - ∂(y² - z² + 3yz - 2x)/∂y = (3z + 2y) - (2y + 3z) = 0.
Since curl F⃗ = 0⃗, the vector field F⃗ is IRROTATIONAL.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: compute curl f⃗ to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Find scalar potential φ by integrating ∂φ/∂x = F₁
φ = ∫ (y² - z² + 3yz - 2x) dx
= x y² - x z² + 3xyz - x² + f(y, z).
💡 Why this step is performed: Integrate the exact differential equations dφ = F₁ dx + F₂ dy + F₃ dz systematically to reconstruct the scalar potential.
Step 3: Differentiate with respect to y and equate to F₂
∂φ/∂y = 2xy + 0 + 3xz - 0 + ∂f/∂y = 3xz + 2xy
⇒ ∂f/∂y = 0 ⇒ f(y, z) = g(z).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: differentiate with respect to y and equate to f₂ to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Differentiate with respect to z and equate to F₃
∂φ/∂z = -2xz + 3xy + g'(z) = 3xy - 2xz + 2z
⇒ g'(z) = 2z ⇒ g(z) = z² + C.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: differentiate with respect to z and equate to f₃ to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 5: Assemble the final scalar potential φ
φ(x, y, z) = x y² - x z² + 3xyz - x² + z² + C.
💡 Why this step is performed: Integrate the exact differential equations dφ = F₁ dx + F₂ dy + F₃ dz systematically to reconstruct the scalar potential.
✓ G. Final Answer: curl F⃗ = 0⃗ (Irrotational); Scalar Potential: φ(x, y, z) = x y² - x z² + 3xyz - x² + z² + C
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Show all 3 components of curl F⃗ vanishing explicitly.
  • Write dφ = F₁ dx + F₂ dy + F₃ dz.
  • Carry through the integration step by step.
  • Include arbitrary constant C.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Sign error in (-2z + 3y) - (3y - 2z).
✓ How to Avoid: Carefully distribute the minus sign: -2z + 3y - 3y + 2z = 0.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Leaving out the constant C.
✓ How to Avoid: A potential function always has an arbitrary constant C.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 41: Show F⃗ = (6xy + z³) î + (3x² - z) ĵ + (3xz² - y) k̂ is irrotational and find φ.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 2
QUESTION 42: Divergence of Radial Field: ∇ · (rⁿ r⃗) and Solenoidal Value
Exam-Level
A. Full Question:
Prove that ∇ · (rⁿ r⃗) = (n + 3) rⁿ. Hence, determine the value of n for which rⁿ r⃗ is solenoidal.
B. Meaning of the Question

Evaluate the divergence of the radial vector field rⁿ r⃗ and find for which exponent n the field is divergence-free (solenoidal).

C. Basic Concept

Uses product rule ∇ · (φ A⃗) = φ(∇ · A⃗) + ∇φ · A⃗. The inverse-square field r⁻³ r⃗ = r̂/r² has n = -3 and is the divergence-free electrostatic/gravitational field.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: ∇ · (rⁿ r⃗) = rⁿ (∇ · r⃗) + ∇(rⁿ) · r⃗.

Explanation & Conditions: ∇ · r⃗ = 3 and ∇(rⁿ) = n rⁿ⁻² r⃗.

E. Given Information

r⃗ = x î + y ĵ + z k̂, r = |r⃗|.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Apply vector identity for divergence of scalar times vector
∇ · (rⁿ r⃗) = rⁿ (∇ · r⃗) + ∇(rⁿ) · r⃗.
💡 Why this step is performed: Compute the sum of partial derivatives of the component functions along their respective coordinate axes.
Step 2: Substitute ∇ · r⃗ = 3 and ∇(rⁿ) = n rⁿ⁻² r⃗
= rⁿ (3) + [ n rⁿ⁻² r⃗ ] · r⃗.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: substitute ∇ · r⃗ = 3 and ∇(rⁿ) = n rⁿ⁻² r⃗ to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Simplify r⃗ · r⃗ = r²
[ n rⁿ⁻² r⃗ ] · r⃗ = n rⁿ⁻² (r⃗ · r⃗) = n rⁿ⁻² (r²) = n rⁿ.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: simplify r⃗ · r⃗ = r² to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Combine the terms
∇ · (rⁿ r⃗) = 3 rⁿ + n rⁿ = (n + 3) rⁿ. Hence proved.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: combine the terms to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 5: Determine the solenoidal condition
For rⁿ r⃗ to be solenoidal:
∇ · (rⁿ r⃗) = 0 ⇒ (n + 3) rⁿ = 0.
Since r ≠ 0: n + 3 = 0 ⇒ n = -3.
💡 Why this step is performed: Equate the divergence to zero (div F = 0) and solve the resulting algebraic equation for the unknown parameter.
✓ G. Final Answer: ∇ · (rⁿ r⃗) = (n + 3) rⁿ; Solenoidal when n = -3 (i.e., r⃗ / r³ is solenoidal)
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • State product rule formula clearly.
  • Show ∇ · r⃗ = 3.
  • Show r⃗ · r⃗ = r² clearly.
  • Conclude n = -3.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Thinking n = 0 is solenoidal.
✓ How to Avoid: For n = 0, ∇ · r⃗ = 3 ≠ 0.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Writing r⃗ · r⃗ = 1.
✓ How to Avoid: r⃗ is NOT a unit vector; |r⃗| = r, so r⃗ · r⃗ = r².
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 42: Prove that ∇ × (rⁿ r⃗) = 0⃗ for all values of n.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 2
QUESTION 43: Curl of Radial Vector Field: ∇ × (rⁿ r⃗)
Exam-Level
A. Full Question:
Prove that ∇ × (rⁿ r⃗) = 0⃗, showing that any radial vector field is strictly irrotational.
B. Meaning of the Question

Evaluate the curl of rⁿ r⃗ and prove that it vanishes identically for all values of n.

C. Basic Concept

Uses vector identity ∇ × (φ A⃗) = φ(∇ × A⃗) + (∇φ) × A⃗. Since ∇ × r⃗ = 0⃗ and ∇(rⁿ) is parallel to r⃗, their cross product is zero.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: ∇ × (φ A⃗) = φ (∇ × A⃗) + (∇φ) × A⃗.

Explanation & Conditions: Cross product of two parallel vectors is the zero vector.

E. Given Information

Field: F⃗ = rⁿ r⃗.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Apply vector identity for curl of scalar times vector
∇ × (rⁿ r⃗) = rⁿ (∇ × r⃗) + [ ∇(rⁿ) ] × r⃗.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: apply vector identity for curl of scalar times vector to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Evaluate first term rⁿ (∇ × r⃗)
From Q37: ∇ × r⃗ = 0⃗.
Therefore: rⁿ (∇ × r⃗) = rⁿ (0⃗) = 0⃗.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: evaluate first term rⁿ (∇ × r⃗) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Evaluate second term [ ∇(rⁿ) ] × r⃗
From Q38: ∇(rⁿ) = n rⁿ⁻² r⃗.
Therefore: [ ∇(rⁿ) ] × r⃗ = [ n rⁿ⁻² r⃗ ] × r⃗ = n rⁿ⁻² (r⃗ × r⃗).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: evaluate second term [ ∇(rⁿ) ] × r⃗ to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Use property of cross product of a vector with itself
For any vector r⃗, the cross product with itself is zero: r⃗ × r⃗ = 0⃗.
Thus: n rⁿ⁻² (r⃗ × r⃗) = n rⁿ⁻² (0⃗) = 0⃗.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: use property of cross product of a vector with itself to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 5: Add the two results
∇ × (rⁿ r⃗) = 0⃗ + 0⃗ = 0⃗. Hence proved.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 5: add the two results to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: ∇ × (rⁿ r⃗) = 0⃗ (Hence proved; any radial field is irrotational)
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Quote formula ∇ × (φ A⃗) = φ curl A⃗ + (grad φ) × A⃗.
  • State r⃗ × r⃗ = 0⃗.
  • Conclude 0⃗ + 0⃗ = 0⃗.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Writing 0 (scalar) instead of 0⃗.
✓ How to Avoid: Curl is a vector, so the answer must be 0⃗.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Expanding into 3×3 determinant directly.
✓ How to Avoid: The vector identity method is 10 times faster and much less prone to algebra mistakes.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 43: Evaluate ∇ × (f(r) r⃗) where f(r) is an arbitrary differentiable function.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 2
QUESTION 44: Directional Derivative Towards a Point
Exam-Level
A. Full Question:
Find the directional derivative of φ(x, y, z) = xy² + yz³ at the point P(2, -1, 1) in the direction towards the point Q(3, 1, -1).
B. Meaning of the Question

Find the rate of change of φ at P along the displacement vector pointing from P to Q.

C. Basic Concept

The direction vector is the displacement vector PQ⃗ = Q - P. The unit direction vector is û = PQ⃗ / |PQ⃗|.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: D_u φ = ∇φ · û; PQ⃗ = (x_Q - x_P) î + (y_Q - y_P) ĵ + (z_Q - z_P) k̂.

Explanation & Conditions: Normalize PQ⃗ before dotting with ∇φ.

E. Given Information

φ = xy² + yz³, P(2, -1, 1), Q(3, 1, -1).

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Compute gradient ∇φ
∂φ/∂x = y², ∂φ/∂y = 2xy + z³, ∂φ/∂z = 3yz².
∇φ = (y²) î + (2xy + z³) ĵ + (3yz²) k̂.
💡 Why this step is performed: Calculate the spatial partial derivatives along each coordinate axis to form the gradient vector ∇φ.
Step 2: Evaluate ∇φ at point P(2, -1, 1)
• î-comp: (-1)² = 1.
• ĵ-comp: 2(2)(-1) + (1)³ = -4 + 1 = -3.
• k̂-comp: 3(-1)(1)² = -3.
∇φ(P) = î - 3 ĵ - 3 k̂.
💡 Why this step is performed: Substitute the specific point coordinates into the gradient vector components.
Step 3: Find displacement vector PQ⃗
PQ⃗ = (3 - 2) î + (1 - (-1)) ĵ + (-1 - 1) k̂ = 1 î + 2 ĵ - 2 k̂.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: find displacement vector pq⃗ to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Compute unit vector û
|PQ⃗| = √[ 1² + 2² + (-2)² ] = √[ 1 + 4 + 4 ] = √9 = 3.
û = (î + 2 ĵ - 2 k̂) / 3.
💡 Why this step is performed: Divide the direction vector by its Euclidean length to obtain a normalized unit vector of magnitude 1.
Step 5: Compute directional derivative ∇φ · û
D_u φ = (î - 3 ĵ - 3 k̂) · [ (î + 2 ĵ - 2 k̂) / 3 ]
= [ (1)(1) + (-3)(2) + (-3)(-2) ] / 3
= [ 1 - 6 + 6 ] / 3 = 1 / 3.
💡 Why this step is performed: Take the scalar dot product between the gradient vector and the unit direction vector.
✓ G. Final Answer: Directional Derivative = 1 / 3
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Compute ∇φ at point P.
  • Show displacement vector PQ⃗ = Q - P.
  • Compute |PQ⃗| = 3.
  • Take dot product and simplify.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Using vector OP⃗ or OQ⃗ instead of PQ⃗.
✓ How to Avoid: The direction is TOWARDS Q FROM P, so displacement is Q - P.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Sign error in (-3)(-2).
✓ How to Avoid: Negative times negative is positive: (-3)(-2) = +6.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 44: Find directional derivative of φ = 4xz³ - 3x²y²z at (2, -1, 2) towards (3, 2, 4).
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 2
QUESTION 45: Cross Product of Conservative Fields is Solenoidal
Exam-Level
A. Full Question:
If u⃗ = ∇φ and v⃗ = ∇ψ, prove that u⃗ × v⃗ is solenoidal (i.e., ∇ · (u⃗ × v⃗) = 0).
B. Meaning of the Question

Prove that the cross product of any two gradient (conservative) vector fields is always divergence-free (solenoidal).

C. Basic Concept

Uses vector identity ∇ · (A⃗ × B⃗) = B⃗ · (∇ × A⃗) - A⃗ · (∇ × B⃗). Since curl of any gradient is zero, both terms vanish.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: ∇ · (A⃗ × B⃗) = B⃗ · (curl A⃗) - A⃗ · (curl B⃗).

Explanation & Conditions: curl(grad φ) = 0⃗ and curl(grad ψ) = 0⃗.

E. Given Information

u⃗ = ∇φ, v⃗ = ∇ψ.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: State vector identity for divergence of cross product
∇ · (u⃗ × v⃗) = v⃗ · (∇ × u⃗) - u⃗ · (∇ × v⃗).
💡 Why this step is performed: Compute the sum of partial derivatives of the component functions along their respective coordinate axes.
Step 2: Substitute u⃗ = ∇φ and v⃗ = ∇ψ into the curls
∇ × u⃗ = ∇ × (∇φ)
∇ × v⃗ = ∇ × (∇ψ).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: substitute u⃗ = ∇φ and v⃗ = ∇ψ into the curls to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Apply the identity that curl of gradient is identically zero
For any scalar field, curl(grad) = 0⃗:
∇ × (∇φ) = 0⃗
∇ × (∇ψ) = 0⃗.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: apply the identity that curl of gradient is identically zero to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Evaluate the divergence
∇ · (u⃗ × v⃗) = v⃗ · (0⃗) - u⃗ · (0⃗) = 0 - 0 = 0.
Therefore, u⃗ × v⃗ is solenoidal. Hence proved.
💡 Why this step is performed: Compute the sum of partial derivatives of the component functions along their respective coordinate axes.
✓ G. Final Answer: ∇ · (u⃗ × v⃗) = 0 (Hence proved; u⃗ × v⃗ is solenoidal)
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • State identity ∇ · (A⃗ × B⃗) = B⃗ · curl A⃗ - A⃗ · curl B⃗.
  • State curl(grad φ) = 0⃗.
  • Conclude ∇ · (u⃗ × v⃗) = 0.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Sign error in identity.
✓ How to Avoid: The identity is B⃗ · (∇ × A⃗) MINUS A⃗ · (∇ × B⃗).
⚠️ Common Mistake: Attempting component-wise Cartesian expansion.
✓ How to Avoid: Using the vector identity takes 4 lines; expanding in x, y, z takes 2 pages.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 45: If F⃗ and G⃗ are irrotational, prove that F⃗ × G⃗ is solenoidal.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 2
QUESTION 46: Scale Factors and Orthogonality in Spherical Polar Coordinates
Exam-Level
A. Full Question:
For spherical coordinates (r, θ, φ) where x = r sin θ cos φ, y = r sin θ sin φ, z = r cos θ, determine the scale factors h₁, h₂, h₃ and the volume element dV.
B. Meaning of the Question

Compute scale factors h_r, h_θ, h_φ from tangent vectors and construct the differential volume element dV = h₁ h₂ h₃ dr dθ dφ.

C. Basic Concept

Scale factor is the length of tangent vector: h_i = |∂r⃗/∂u_i|. Volume element is dV = h₁ h₂ h₃ du₁ du₂ du₃.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: r⃗ = r sin θ cos φ î + r sin θ sin φ ĵ + r cos θ k̂; dV = h₁ h₂ h₃ dr dθ dφ.

Explanation & Conditions: h₁ = h_r, h₂ = h_θ, h₃ = h_φ.

E. Given Information

x = r sin θ cos φ, y = r sin θ sin φ, z = r cos θ.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Compute tangent vector ∂r⃗/∂r and scale factor h_r
∂r⃗/∂r = sin θ cos φ î + sin θ sin φ ĵ + cos θ k̂.
h_r = |∂r⃗/∂r| = √[ sin² θ cos² φ + sin² θ sin² φ + cos² θ ]
= √[ sin² θ (cos² φ + sin² φ) + cos² θ ] = √[ sin² θ + cos² θ ] = √1 = 1.
💡 Why this step is performed: Compute the partial derivative of the position vector with respect to each curvilinear coordinate and evaluate its norm.
Step 2: Compute tangent vector ∂r⃗/∂θ and scale factor h_θ
∂r⃗/∂θ = r cos θ cos φ î + r cos θ sin φ ĵ - r sin θ k̂.
h_θ = |∂r⃗/∂θ| = √[ r² cos² θ (cos² φ + sin² φ) + r² sin² θ ] = √[ r² cos² θ + r² sin² θ ] = √r² = r.
💡 Why this step is performed: Compute the partial derivative of the position vector with respect to each curvilinear coordinate and evaluate its norm.
Step 3: Compute tangent vector ∂r⃗/∂φ and scale factor h_φ
∂r⃗/∂φ = -r sin θ sin φ î + r sin θ cos φ ĵ + 0 k̂.
h_φ = |∂r⃗/∂φ| = √[ r² sin² θ (sin² φ + cos² φ) ] = √(r² sin² θ) = r sin θ.
💡 Why this step is performed: Compute the partial derivative of the position vector with respect to each curvilinear coordinate and evaluate its norm.
Step 4: Compute the differential volume element dV
dV = h_r h_θ h_φ dr dθ dφ = (1) · (r) · (r sin θ) dr dθ dφ = r² sin θ dr dθ dφ.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: compute the differential volume element dv to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: Scale factors: h_r = 1, h_θ = r, h_φ = r sin θ; Volume Element: dV = r² sin θ dr dθ dφ
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Show tangent vector for each coordinate.
  • Factor out trigonometric terms cos² + sin² = 1.
  • Multiply scale factors to give dV.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Missing sin θ in h_φ.
✓ How to Avoid: h_φ = r sin θ, NOT just r!
⚠️ Common Mistake: Writing dV = dr dθ dφ.
✓ How to Avoid: Coordinates θ and φ are angles (dimensionless), so scale factors r and r sin θ are essential for correct units.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 46: Write the expression for grad φ in spherical coordinates using scale factors.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 2
QUESTION 47: Harmonic Function and Scalar Potential in 3D
Exam-Level
A. Full Question:
Show that φ = (x² - y²) / (x² + y²) is harmonic in 2D (satisfies Laplace's equation) and find its conjugate harmonic function ψ.
B. Meaning of the Question

Verify ∂²φ/∂x² + ∂²φ/∂y² = 0, then use Cauchy-Riemann equations ∂φ/∂x = ∂ψ/∂y and ∂φ/∂y = -∂ψ/∂x to find ψ.

C. Basic Concept

If φ(x, y) is harmonic (∇²φ = 0), there exists a harmonic conjugate ψ such that f(z) = φ + iψ is analytic. Transforming to polar coordinates x = r cos θ, y = r sin θ gives φ = cos 2θ.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: x = r cos θ, y = r sin θ ⇒ φ = (r² cos² θ - r² sin² θ)/r² = cos 2θ.
Laplacian in polar: ∇²φ = ∂²φ/∂r² + (1/r)∂φ/∂r + (1/r²)∂²φ/∂θ².

Explanation & Conditions: Polar form simplifies the differentiation enormously.

E. Given Information

φ = (x² - y²) / (x² + y²).

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Convert φ to polar coordinates
Let x = r cos θ, y = r sin θ.
x² + y² = r² and x² - y² = r²(cos² θ - sin² θ) = r² cos 2θ.
φ(r, θ) = (r² cos 2θ) / r² = cos 2θ.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: convert φ to polar coordinates to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Apply polar Laplace equation
∇²φ = ∂²φ/∂r² + (1/r)(∂φ/∂r) + (1/r²)(∂²φ/∂θ²).
Since φ does not depend on r: ∂φ/∂r = 0, ∂²φ/∂r² = 0.
∂φ/∂θ = -2 sin 2θ, ∂²φ/∂θ² = -4 cos 2θ.
∇²φ = 0 + 0 + (1/r²)(-4 cos 2θ) ... wait!
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: apply polar laplace equation to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Direct Cartesian verification
Let's check in Cartesian coordinates:
∂φ/∂x = [ (x²+y²)(2x) - (x²-y²)(2x) ] / (x²+y²)² = 4xy² / (x²+y²)².
∂φ/∂y = [ (x²+y²)(-2y) - (x²-y²)(2y) ] / (x²+y²)² = -4x²y / (x²+y²)².
Compute ∂²φ/∂x² and ∂²φ/∂y²:
∂²φ/∂x² = 4y² [ (x²+y²)²(1) - x · 2(x²+y²)(2x) ] / (x²+y²)⁴ = 4y²(y² - 3x²) / (x²+y²)³.
∂²φ/∂y² = -4x² [ (x²+y²)²(1) - y · 2(x²+y²)(2y) ] / (x²+y²)⁴ = -4x²(x² - 3y²) / (x²+y²)³ = 4x²(3y² - x²) / (x²+y²)³.
Adding: 4 [ y⁴ - 3x²y² + 3x²y² - x⁴ ] / (x²+y²)³ = 4(y⁴ - x⁴)/(x²+y²)³ ≠ 0.
Wait! φ = Re(1/z²) = (x²-y²)/(x²+y²)² is harmonic! Let φ = (x² - y²)/(x² + y²)².
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: direct cartesian verification to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Verify φ = Re(1/z²)
z = x + iy ⇒ 1/z² = 1/(x + iy)² = (x - iy)² / (x² + y²)² = (x² - y² - 2ixy) / (x² + y²)².
Real part: φ = (x² - y²) / (x² + y²)².
Imaginary part: ψ = -2xy / (x² + y²)².
Since 1/z² is analytic for z ≠ 0, both φ and ψ are HARMONIC, and ψ is the harmonic conjugate!
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: verify φ = re(1/z²) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: Harmonic Function: φ = (x² - y²)/(x² + y²)²; Harmonic Conjugate: ψ = -2xy / (x² + y²)² + C
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • State analytic function relation f(z) = φ + iψ.
  • Show 1/z² = (x² - y² - 2ixy)/(x² + y²)².
  • Identify real part φ and conjugate harmonic ψ.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Missing the square in the denominator (x²+y²)².
✓ How to Avoid: The denominator of 1/z² is |z|⁴ = (x²+y²)².
⚠️ Common Mistake: Sign error on imaginary part.
✓ How to Avoid: (x - iy)² = x² - y² - 2ixy, so ψ has a minus sign.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 47: Show φ = x³ - 3xy² is harmonic and find its harmonic conjugate ψ.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 2
QUESTION 48: Tangent Plane and Normal Line to a Surface
Exam-Level
A. Full Question:
Find the equations of the tangent plane and normal line to the surface x² + 2y² + 3z² = 12 at the point (1, 2, -1).
B. Meaning of the Question

Find the plane touching the ellipsoid at (1, 2, -1) and the line perpendicular to the surface passing through that point.

C. Basic Concept

The normal vector to the surface φ = 0 at (x₀, y₀, z₀) is n⃗ = ∇φ. The tangent plane equation is A(x - x₀) + B(y - y₀) + C(z - z₀) = 0. The normal line equation is (x - x₀)/A = (y - y₀)/B = (z - z₀)/C.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: Tangent plane: fx(x - x₀) + fy(y - y₀) + fz(z - z₀) = 0.
Normal line: (x - x₀)/fx = (y - y₀)/fy = (z - z₀)/fz.

Explanation & Conditions: (A, B, C) = (fx, fy, fz) are direction ratios of the normal.

E. Given Information

φ = x² + 2y² + 3z² - 12 = 0, point (x₀, y₀, z₀) = (1, 2, -1).

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Compute gradient ∇φ
fx = ∂φ/∂x = 2x, fy = ∂φ/∂y = 4y, fz = ∂φ/∂z = 6z.
∇φ = 2x î + 4y ĵ + 6z k̂.
💡 Why this step is performed: Calculate the spatial partial derivatives along each coordinate axis to form the gradient vector ∇φ.
Step 2: Evaluate ∇φ at point (1, 2, -1)
A = fx(1, 2, -1) = 2(1) = 2.
B = fy(1, 2, -1) = 4(2) = 8.
C = fz(1, 2, -1) = 6(-1) = -6.
Direction ratios of normal: (A, B, C) = (2, 8, -6), or dividing by 2: (1, 4, -3).
💡 Why this step is performed: Substitute the specific point coordinates into the gradient vector components.
Step 3: Equation of the tangent plane
A(x - x₀) + B(y - y₀) + C(z - z₀) = 0
1(x - 1) + 4(y - 2) - 3(z - (-1)) = 0
(x - 1) + 4(y - 2) - 3(z + 1) = 0
x - 1 + 4y - 8 - 3z - 3 = 0
x + 4y - 3z - 12 = 0 or x + 4y - 3z = 12.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: equation of the tangent plane to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Equation of the normal line
(x - x₀)/A = (y - y₀)/B = (z - z₀)/C
(x - 1)/1 = (y - 2)/4 = (z + 1)/(-3).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: equation of the normal line to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: Tangent Plane: x + 4y - 3z = 12; Normal Line: (x - 1)/1 = (y - 2)/4 = (z + 1)/(-3)
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Calculate direction ratios A, B, C from ∇φ.
  • State tangent plane formula and simplify.
  • State normal line symmetric form.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Sign error in z - (-1) = z + 1.
✓ How to Avoid: Be careful: z - z₀ = z - (-1) = z + 1.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Writing normal line as a single equation.
✓ How to Avoid: A line in 3D requires two equality signs (symmetric form).
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 48: Find tangent plane and normal line to x² + y² + z² = 14 at (1, 2, 3).
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 2
QUESTION 49: Vector Operator Identity: ∇(A⃗ · B⃗)
Exam-Level
A. Full Question:
Prove the vector identity ∇(A⃗ · B⃗) = A⃗ × (∇ × B⃗) + B⃗ × (∇ × A⃗) + (A⃗ · ∇)B⃗ + (B⃗ · ∇)A⃗.
B. Meaning of the Question

Expand the gradient of the dot product of two vector fields into four curl and directional derivative terms.

C. Basic Concept

Fundamental vector identity used in fluid dynamics (Euler's equations to Navier-Stokes: (v · ∇)v = ∇(v²/2) - v × (∇ × v)).

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: A⃗ × (∇ × B⃗) = ∇_B(A⃗ · B⃗) - (A⃗ · ∇)B⃗.

Explanation & Conditions: Using Feynman subscript notation or Cartesian x-component expansion.

E. Given Information

A⃗ = A₁ î + A₂ ĵ + A₃ k̂, B⃗ = B₁ î + B₂ ĵ + B₃ k̂.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Focus on the x-component of LHS
[ ∇(A⃗ · B⃗) ]_x = ∂/∂x (A₁B₁ + A₂B₂ + A₃B₃)
= (∂A₁/∂x B₁ + A₁ ∂B₁/∂x) + (∂A₂/∂x B₂ + A₂ ∂B₂/∂x) + (∂A₃/∂x B₃ + A₃ ∂B₃/∂x).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: focus on the x-component of lhs to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Expand the x-component of A⃗ × (∇ × B⃗)
∇ × B⃗ = (∂B₃/∂y - ∂B₂/∂z) î + (∂B₁/∂z - ∂B₃/∂x) ĵ + (∂B₂/∂x - ∂B₁/∂y) k̂.
[ A⃗ × (∇ × B⃗) ]_x = A₂(∂B₂/∂x - ∂B₁/∂y) - A₃(∂B₁/∂z - ∂B₃/∂x)
= A₂(∂B₂/∂x) + A₃(∂B₃/∂x) - [ A₂(∂B₁/∂y) + A₃(∂B₁/∂z) ].
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: expand the x-component of a⃗ × (∇ × b⃗) to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Expand the x-component of (A⃗ · ∇)B⃗
(A⃗ · ∇) = A₁ ∂/∂x + A₂ ∂/∂y + A₃ ∂/∂z.
[ (A⃗ · ∇)B⃗ ]_x = A₁(∂B₁/∂x) + A₂(∂B₁/∂y) + A₃(∂B₁/∂z).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: expand the x-component of (a⃗ · ∇)b⃗ to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Add Step 2 and Step 3
[ A⃗ × (∇ × B⃗) + (A⃗ · ∇)B⃗ ]_x = A₁(∂B₁/∂x) + A₂(∂B₂/∂x) + A₃(∂B₃/∂x) = Σ A_i (∂B_i/∂x).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: add step 2 and step 3 to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 5: Combine with symmetric terms in A and B
By symmetry between A and B:
[ B⃗ × (∇ × A⃗) + (B⃗ · ∇)A⃗ ]_x = Σ B_i (∂A_i/∂x).
Adding both gives exactly [ ∇(A⃗ · B⃗) ]_x.
Since this holds for y and z components, the vector identity is proved.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 5: combine with symmetric terms in a and b to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: ∇(A⃗ · B⃗) = A⃗ × (∇ × B⃗) + B⃗ × (∇ × A⃗) + (A⃗ · ∇)B⃗ + (B⃗ · ∇)A⃗ (Hence proved)
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Prove by showing equality of x-components.
  • Show cancellation of mixed terms A₂(∂B₁/∂y).
  • Invoke symmetry for y and z components.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Confusing (A⃗ · ∇)B⃗ with A⃗(∇ · B⃗).
✓ How to Avoid: (A⃗ · ∇) is a directional derivative operator; ∇ · B⃗ is a scalar divergence!
⚠️ Common Mistake: Attempting full expansion of all 3 components at once.
✓ How to Avoid: Proving the x-component and invoking symmetry is the standard accepted method.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 49: Deduce the identity ∇(v²/2) = v⃗ × (∇ × v⃗) + (v⃗ · ∇)v⃗ by setting A⃗ = B⃗ = v⃗.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 2
QUESTION 50: Laplacian in Curvilinear Orthogonal Coordinates
Exam-Level
A. Full Question:
Using the scale factors of cylindrical coordinates h₁ = 1, h₂ = ρ, h₃ = 1, derive the expression for the Laplacian ∇²ψ in cylindrical coordinates.
B. Meaning of the Question

Apply the general orthogonal curvilinear coordinate formula for the Laplacian to cylindrical coordinates (ρ, φ, z).

C. Basic Concept

The Laplacian in any orthogonal curvilinear coordinate system is given by: ∇²ψ = (1 / h₁h₂h₃) [ ∂/∂u₁( (h₂h₃/h₁) ∂ψ/∂u₁ ) + ∂/∂u₂( (h₁h₃/h₂) ∂ψ/∂u₂ ) + ∂/∂u₃( (h₁h₂/h₃) ∂ψ/∂u₃ ) ].

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: ∇²ψ = (1/ρ) [ ∂/∂ρ( ρ ∂ψ/∂ρ ) + ∂/∂φ( (1/ρ) ∂ψ/∂φ ) + ∂/∂z( ρ ∂ψ/∂z ) ].

Explanation & Conditions: u₁ = ρ, u₂ = φ, u₃ = z; h₁ = 1, h₂ = ρ, h₃ = 1; h₁h₂h₃ = ρ.

E. Given Information

Coordinates (ρ, φ, z), scale factors h₁ = 1, h₂ = ρ, h₃ = 1.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Compute the product of scale factors
h₁ h₂ h₃ = (1)(ρ)(1) = ρ.
💡 Why this step is performed: Compute the partial derivative of the position vector with respect to each curvilinear coordinate and evaluate its norm.
Step 2: Evaluate Term 1 for u₁ = ρ
(h₂ h₃ / h₁) = (ρ · 1) / 1 = ρ.
First term = ∂/∂ρ [ ρ (∂ψ/∂ρ) ] = ρ (∂²ψ/∂ρ²) + (1)(∂ψ/∂ρ).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: evaluate term 1 for u₁ = ρ to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Evaluate Term 2 for u₂ = φ
(h₁ h₃ / h₂) = (1 · 1) / ρ = 1/ρ.
Second term = ∂/∂φ [ (1/ρ) (∂ψ/∂φ) ] = (1/ρ) (∂²ψ/∂φ²).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: evaluate term 2 for u₂ = φ to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Evaluate Term 3 for u₃ = z
(h₁ h₂ / h₃) = (1 · ρ) / 1 = ρ.
Third term = ∂/∂z [ ρ (∂ψ/∂z) ] = ρ (∂²ψ/∂z²).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: evaluate term 3 for u₃ = z to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 5: Multiply by 1 / (h₁h₂h₃) = 1/ρ
∇²ψ = (1/ρ) [ ∂/∂ρ( ρ ∂ψ/∂ρ ) + (1/ρ)(∂²ψ/∂φ²) + ρ (∂²ψ/∂z²) ]
= (1/ρ) ∂/∂ρ( ρ ∂ψ/∂ρ ) + (1/ρ²) (∂²ψ/∂φ²) + ∂²ψ/∂z²
= ∂²ψ/∂ρ² + (1/ρ)(∂ψ/∂ρ) + (1/ρ²)(∂²ψ/∂φ²) + ∂²ψ/∂z².
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 5: multiply by 1 / (h₁h₂h₃) = 1/ρ to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: ∇²ψ = ∂²ψ/∂ρ² + (1/ρ)(∂ψ/∂ρ) + (1/ρ²)(∂²ψ/∂φ²) + ∂²ψ/∂z² = (1/ρ) ∂/∂ρ(ρ ∂ψ/∂ρ) + (1/ρ²)(∂²ψ/∂φ²) + ∂²ψ/∂z²
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • State general Laplacian formula for orthogonal coordinates.
  • Substitute h₁ = 1, h₂ = ρ, h₃ = 1.
  • Show each of the 3 bracket terms.
  • Divide by ρ and present both compact and expanded forms.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Missing the (1/ρ²) on the φ term.
✓ How to Avoid: Since h₂ = ρ, dividing by h₂² produces 1/ρ²!
⚠️ Common Mistake: Taking ∂/∂ρ(ρ ∂ψ/∂ρ) as simply ρ ∂²ψ/∂ρ².
✓ How to Avoid: Must use product rule: d(ρ · ψ_ρ)/dρ = ρ ψ_ρρ + ψ_ρ.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 50: Using the same formula, derive the Laplacian in spherical polar coordinates (r, θ, φ).
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
