VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 3
QUESTION 51: Rank of a 2×2 and 3×3 Matrix by Elementary Row Operations
Fundamental
A. Full Question:
Find the rank of the matrix A = [[1, 2, 3], [2, 4, 6], [3, 6, 9]] by reducing it to row-echelon form.
B. Meaning of the Question

Transform matrix A into an upper triangular row-echelon form using elementary row operations and count the number of non-zero rows, which equals the rank.

C. Basic Concept

The rank of a matrix is the maximum number of linearly independent rows (or columns). Elementary row operations do not alter the rank.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: Rank ρ(A) = Number of non-zero rows in row-echelon form.

Explanation & Conditions: A non-zero row contains at least one non-zero entry.

E. Given Information

A = [[1, 2, 3], [2, 4, 6], [3, 6, 9]].

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Write the initial matrix
A = [ [1, 2, 3], [2, 4, 6], [3, 6, 9] ]. Pivot in row 1 is 1.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: write the initial matrix to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Apply row operations to eliminate entries below pivot
R₂ → R₂ - 2R₁:
[2 - 2(1), 4 - 2(2), 6 - 2(3)] = [0, 0, 0].
R₃ → R₃ - 3R₁:
[3 - 3(1), 6 - 3(2), 9 - 3(3)] = [0, 0, 0].
💡 Why this step is performed: Apply elementary row operations to introduce zeros below the pivot element and reduce the matrix toward echelon form.
Step 3: State the reduced row-echelon matrix
A ~ [ [1, 2, 3], [0, 0, 0], [0, 0, 0] ].
💡 Why this step is performed: Apply elementary row operations to introduce zeros below the pivot element and reduce the matrix toward echelon form.
Step 4: Count non-zero rows to find rank
There is only 1 non-zero row: [1, 2, 3]. Rows 2 and 3 are entirely zero.
Therefore, Rank ρ(A) = 1.
💡 Why this step is performed: Count the number of non-zero rows in the row-echelon form; this count equals the rank of the matrix.
✓ G. Final Answer: Rank ρ(A) = 1
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • State row operations R₂ → R₂ - 2R₁ and R₃ → R₃ - 3R₁ explicitly.
  • Show intermediate matrices.
  • State definition: Rank = number of non-zero rows = 1.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Saying rank is 0 because two rows became zero.
✓ How to Avoid: Rank is the number of NON-ZERO rows, so it is 1, not 0!
⚠️ Common Mistake: Using column operations when asked for row-echelon form.
✓ How to Avoid: Row-echelon form strictly requires ROW operations.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 51: Find rank of A = [[1, -1, 2], [2, -2, 4], [-1, 1, -2]].
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 3
QUESTION 52: Characteristic Equation and Eigenvalues of a 2×2 Matrix
Fundamental
A. Full Question:
Find the characteristic equation and eigenvalues of the matrix A = [[4, 1], [2, 3]].
B. Meaning of the Question

Set up the characteristic polynomial det(A - λI) = 0 and solve the quadratic equation for the eigenvalues λ.

C. Basic Concept

Eigenvalues λ are scalars such that A v = λ v for some non-zero vector v. They represent the scale factors by which eigenvector directions are stretched or flipped.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: |A - λI| = 0 ⇒ λ² - (Trace A) λ + det(A) = 0.

Explanation & Conditions: Trace(A) = sum of main diagonal entries; det(A) = ad - bc.

E. Given Information

A = [[4, 1], [2, 3]].

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Compute Trace and Determinant of A
Trace(A) = 4 + 3 = 7.
det(A) = (4)(3) - (1)(2) = 12 - 2 = 10.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: compute trace and determinant of a to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Write the characteristic equation
λ² - (Trace A) λ + det(A) = 0
λ² - 7λ + 10 = 0.
💡 Why this step is performed: Set up the characteristic determinant det(A - λI) = 0 and expand to obtain the characteristic polynomial.
Step 3: Factorize the quadratic polynomial
(λ - 2)(λ - 5) = 0.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: factorize the quadratic polynomial to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Solve for eigenvalues λ
λ₁ = 2, λ₂ = 5.
💡 Why this step is performed: Find the roots of the characteristic polynomial to determine all scalar eigenvalues.
Step 5: Verify with properties
Sum of eigenvalues = 2 + 5 = 7 = Trace(A). Verified!
Product of eigenvalues = 2 · 5 = 10 = det(A). Verified!
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 5: verify with properties to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: Characteristic Equation: λ² - 7λ + 10 = 0; Eigenvalues: λ = 2, 5
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • State formula |A - λI| = 0.
  • Show Trace and Determinant calculations.
  • Factorize quadratic equation.
  • Verify using sum and product properties.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Sign error in quadratic: writing +7λ instead of -7λ.
✓ How to Avoid: Formula is λ² MINUS (Trace)λ PLUS det = 0.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Arithmetic error in det: 4(3) + 1(2).
✓ How to Avoid: Determinant formula is ad - bc.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 52: Find eigenvalues of A = [[5, 4], [1, 2]].
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 3
QUESTION 53: Consistency Criteria for Linear Systems (Rouché-Capelli Theorem)
Fundamental
A. Full Question:
State the Rouché-Capelli theorem for the system of linear equations AX = B with n variables, and classify the types of solutions based on rank.
B. Meaning of the Question

Explain the necessary and sufficient rank conditions for a non-homogeneous linear system AX = B to have unique, infinitely many, or no solutions.

C. Basic Concept

The augmented matrix [A | B] combines coefficients and constants. The system is solvable if and only if appending B does not increase the rank of A.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: Let ρ(A) = rank of coefficient matrix, ρ([A|B]) = rank of augmented matrix, n = number of unknowns.

Explanation & Conditions: • Inconsistent (No solution): ρ(A) ≠ ρ([A|B]).
• Consistent: ρ(A) = ρ([A|B]) = r.
- Unique solution: r = n.
- Infinitely many solutions: r < n (with n - r free parameters).

E. Given Information

System AX = B, coefficient matrix A (m×n), augmented matrix [A|B] (m×(n+1)).

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: State Condition for Inconsistency (No Solution)
If ρ(A) < ρ([A|B]), the system is INCONSISTENT and has NO SOLUTION.
In echelon form, this corresponds to a row of the form [0, 0, ..., 0 | k] where k ≠ 0 (e.g., 0 = k, which is impossible).
💡 Why this step is performed: Compare the rank of the coefficient matrix ρ(A) with the augmented matrix ρ([A|B]) using the Rouché-Capelli criteria.
Step 2: State Condition for Unique Solution
If ρ(A) = ρ([A|B]) = n (where n is the number of unknowns), the system is CONSISTENT and has a UNIQUE SOLUTION.
Every column has a pivot; no free variables exist.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: state condition for unique solution to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: State Condition for Infinitely Many Solutions
If ρ(A) = ρ([A|B]) = r < n, the system is CONSISTENT and has INFINITELY MANY SOLUTIONS.
There are (n - r) free parameters (independent arbitrary constants).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: state condition for infinitely many solutions to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: Rouché-Capelli Theorem: (i) Inconsistent: ρ(A) ≠ ρ([A|B]); (ii) Unique: ρ(A) = ρ([A|B]) = n; (iii) Infinite: ρ(A) = ρ([A|B]) = r < n with (n-r) arbitrary parameters.
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Draw a clear summary table of the 3 cases.
  • Define n as the number of unknowns.
  • Mention the number of free parameters (n - r).
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Confusing m (number of equations) with n (number of unknowns).
✓ How to Avoid: Always compare rank r with n (number of variables/columns of A)!
⚠️ Common Mistake: Claiming ρ(A) > ρ([A|B]).
✓ How to Avoid: Appending a column cannot decrease rank, so ρ(A) ≤ ρ([A|B]) always.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 53: If A is a 3×4 matrix and ρ(A) = ρ([A|B]) = 2, how many arbitrary parameters does the solution possess?
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 3
QUESTION 54: Homogeneous Linear System AX = 0 (Trivial vs Non-Trivial Solutions)
Fundamental
A. Full Question:
For a homogeneous system AX = 0 of 3 equations in 3 variables, state the condition for a non-trivial (non-zero) solution and solve [[1, 2, 3], [2, 4, 6], [1, 1, 1]] X = 0.
B. Meaning of the Question

Explain when AX = 0 has solutions other than X = 0, and find the solution for the given coefficient matrix.

C. Basic Concept

For homogeneous systems, X = 0 (all zeros) is always a solution, called the trivial solution. Non-trivial solutions exist if and only if det(A) = 0 (or rank ρ(A) < n).

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: Non-trivial solution condition: det(A) = 0 or rank ρ(A) < n.

Explanation & Conditions: If det(A) ≠ 0, only the trivial solution X = [0, 0, 0]ᵀ exists.

E. Given Information

A = [[1, 2, 3], [2, 4, 6], [1, 1, 1]], AX = 0.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Reduce A to echelon form
A = [ [1, 2, 3], [2, 4, 6], [1, 1, 1] ].
R₂ → R₂ - 2R₁: [0, 0, 0].
R₃ → R₃ - R₁: [0, -1, -2].
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: reduce a to echelon form to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Swap R₂ and R₃ to form row-echelon form
A ~ [ [1, 2, 3], [0, -1, -2], [0, 0, 0] ].
Number of non-zero rows = 2. Rank ρ(A) = 2.
Number of variables n = 3.
💡 Why this step is performed: Apply elementary row operations to introduce zeros below the pivot element and reduce the matrix toward echelon form.
Step 3: Determine number of free parameters
Number of free parameters = n - r = 3 - 2 = 1 free parameter (let z = k).
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: determine number of free parameters to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Back-substitute to find x and y in terms of k
From Row 2: -y - 2z = 0 ⇒ y = -2z = -2k.
From Row 1: x + 2y + 3z = 0 ⇒ x + 2(-2k) + 3k = 0 ⇒ x - 4k + 3k = 0 ⇒ x - k = 0 ⇒ x = k.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: back-substitute to find x and y in terms of k to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: Non-trivial solution condition: det(A) = 0 (or rank < n); Solution: X = [k, -2k, k]ᵀ = k [1, -2, 1]ᵀ, where k is any non-zero real number.
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • State condition det(A) = 0 clearly.
  • Row reduce coefficient matrix.
  • Express solution as vector multiplied by parameter k.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Saying k = 0 is a non-trivial solution.
✓ How to Avoid: If k = 0, X = 0 which is the TRIVIAL solution. Non-trivial means k ≠ 0.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Forgetting to introduce the parameter k.
✓ How to Avoid: When rank < n, one or more variables MUST be assigned free parameters.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 54: Determine if [[1, 1, 1], [2, 2, 2], [3, 3, 3]] X = 0 has non-trivial solutions and find them.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 3
QUESTION 55: Properties of Eigenvalues (Trace and Determinant)
Fundamental
A. Full Question:
If the sum of eigenvalues of a 3×3 matrix A is 9 and their product is 24, and one eigenvalue is λ₁ = 2, find the other two eigenvalues.
B. Meaning of the Question

Use the fundamental properties that sum of eigenvalues equals trace and product of eigenvalues equals determinant to find the unknown eigenvalues.

C. Basic Concept

For any n×n matrix: (1) Σ λ_i = Trace(A), (2) Π λ_i = det(A). This provides two algebraic equations connecting the eigenvalues.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: λ₁ + λ₂ + λ₃ = 9; λ₁ · λ₂ · λ₃ = 24; with λ₁ = 2.

Explanation & Conditions: Trace and determinant properties reduce higher degree polynomials to simple quadratics.

E. Given Information

λ₁ + λ₂ + λ₃ = 9, λ₁ λ₂ λ₃ = 24, λ₁ = 2.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Substitute λ₁ = 2 into the sum equation
2 + λ₂ + λ₃ = 9 ⇒ λ₂ + λ₃ = 9 - 2 = 7.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: substitute λ₁ = 2 into the sum equation to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Substitute λ₁ = 2 into the product equation
2 · λ₂ · λ₃ = 24 ⇒ λ₂ · λ₃ = 24 / 2 = 12.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: substitute λ₁ = 2 into the product equation to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Form the quadratic equation for λ₂ and λ₃
The two numbers whose sum is 7 and product is 12 are roots of:
t² - (sum) t + (product) = 0
t² - 7t + 12 = 0.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 3: form the quadratic equation for λ₂ and λ₃ to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 4: Factorize and solve
(t - 3)(t - 4) = 0 ⇒ t = 3 or t = 4.
Therefore, λ₂ = 3 and λ₃ = 4.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 4: factorize and solve to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: The other two eigenvalues are λ₂ = 3 and λ₃ = 4 (All three eigenvalues are 2, 3, 4)
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • State Trace property: Σ λ_i = Trace.
  • State Determinant property: Π λ_i = det.
  • Form quadratic t² - 7t + 12 = 0 and solve.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Guessing roots without showing quadratic equation.
✓ How to Avoid: Always form t² - (sum)t + prod = 0 to show mathematical rigor.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Arithmetic mistake in 24 / 2 = 12.
✓ How to Avoid: Check division carefully.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 55: Two eigenvalues of a 3×3 matrix with Trace = 6 and det = 6 are 1 and 2. Find the third eigenvalue.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 3
QUESTION 56: Rank of a 3×4 Matrix by Echelon Form
Intermediate
A. Full Question:
Find the rank of the matrix A = [[1, 2, -1, 4], [2, 4, 3, 4], [1, 2, 3, 4]] by reducing to row-echelon form.
B. Meaning of the Question

Apply elementary row operations to reduce this 3×4 rectangular matrix to row-echelon form and determine its rank.

C. Basic Concept

For an m×n matrix, rank ≤ min(m, n). Here rank ≤ min(3, 4) = 3.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: Rank = number of non-zero rows in row-echelon form.

Explanation & Conditions: Pivot elements must step to the right in each successive row.

E. Given Information

A = [[1, 2, -1, 4], [2, 4, 3, 4], [1, 2, 3, 4]].

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Eliminate entries below pivot a₁₁ = 1
R₂ → R₂ - 2R₁:
[2 - 2(1), 4 - 2(2), 3 - 2(-1), 4 - 2(4)] = [0, 0, 5, -4].
R₃ → R₃ - R₁:
[1 - 1, 2 - 2, 3 - (-1), 4 - 4] = [0, 0, 4, 0].
💡 Why this step is performed: Apply elementary row operations to introduce zeros below the pivot element and reduce the matrix toward echelon form.
Step 2: Matrix after first sweep
A ~ [ [1, 2, -1, 4], [0, 0, 5, -4], [0, 0, 4, 0] ].
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 2: matrix after first sweep to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 3: Eliminate entry below pivot in column 3
R₃ → 5R₃ - 4R₂:
[0, 0, 5(4) - 4(5), 5(0) - 4(-4)] = [0, 0, 0, 16].
💡 Why this step is performed: Apply elementary row operations to introduce zeros below the pivot element and reduce the matrix toward echelon form.
Step 4: Examine row-echelon form
A ~ [ [1, 2, -1, 4], [0, 0, 5, -4], [0, 0, 0, 16] ].
All 3 rows contain non-zero elements (Row 1 has 1, Row 2 has 5, Row 3 has 16).
Number of non-zero rows = 3.
Therefore, Rank ρ(A) = 3.
💡 Why this step is performed: Apply elementary row operations to introduce zeros below the pivot element and reduce the matrix toward echelon form.
✓ G. Final Answer: Rank ρ(A) = 3
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • Show each row operation with intermediate arithmetic.
  • State the final echelon matrix clearly.
  • Count non-zero rows = 3.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Arithmetic error in 4 - 2(-1) = 6 instead of 3 - (-2) = 5.
✓ How to Avoid: Double minus is positive: 3 - 2(-1) = 3 + 2 = 5.
⚠️ Common Mistake: Trying to make column 2 pivot.
✓ How to Avoid: Column 2 has all zeros below row 1, so the pivot moves to column 3.
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 56: Find rank of A = [[1, 1, 1, 1], [1, 2, 3, -4], [2, 3, 5, -5]].
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
VTU 1BMATS101: APPLIED MATHEMATICS-I MODULE 3
QUESTION 57: Solving Linear System by Gauss Elimination (Unique Solution)
Intermediate
A. Full Question:
Solve the system of equations using Gauss Elimination method:
2x + y + z = 10,
3x + 2y + 3z = 18,
x + 4y + 9z = 16.
B. Meaning of the Question

Convert the augmented matrix [A|B] to upper triangular row-echelon form using elementary row operations, then solve for x, y, z by back-substitution.

C. Basic Concept

Gauss elimination reduces the augmented matrix [A|B] to row-echelon form, transforming a coupled system into a triangular system that is solved from bottom to top.

D. Formula and Explanation

Formula: [A | B] ~ [U | B'] where U is upper triangular.

Explanation & Conditions: Back-substitution: solve z first, then y, then x.

E. Given Information

System of 3 equations in 3 variables.

F. Complete Step-by-Step Solution
Step 1: Set up Augmented Matrix and swap R₁ and R₃ for pivot 1
Augmented matrix [A|B]:
[ [2, 1, 1 | 10], [3, 2, 3 | 18], [1, 4, 9 | 16] ].
Swap R₁ ↔ R₃ to get pivot 1 in (1, 1):
[ [1, 4, 9 | 16], [3, 2, 3 | 18], [2, 1, 1 | 10] ].
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 1: set up augmented matrix and swap r₁ and r₃ for pivot 1 to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
Step 2: Eliminate x from Row 2 and Row 3
R₂ → R₂ - 3R₁:
[3-3, 2-12, 3-27 | 18-48] = [0, -10, -24 | -30] ⇒ divide by -2: [0, 5, 12 | 15].
R₃ → R₃ - 2R₁:
[2-2, 1-8, 1-18 | 10-32] = [0, -7, -17 | -22].
💡 Why this step is performed: Apply elementary row operations to introduce zeros below the pivot element and reduce the matrix toward echelon form.
Step 3: Eliminate y from Row 3
Current matrix:
[ [1, 4, 9 | 16], [0, 5, 12 | 15], [0, -7, -17 | -22] ].
R₃ → 5R₃ + 7R₂:
[0, -35 + 35, 5(-17) + 7(12) | 5(-22) + 7(15)]
= [0, 0, -85 + 84 | -110 + 105] = [0, 0, -1 | -5] ⇒ [0, 0, 1 | 5].
💡 Why this step is performed: Apply elementary row operations to introduce zeros below the pivot element and reduce the matrix toward echelon form.
Step 4: Back-substitution
From Row 3: z = 5.
From Row 2: 5y + 12z = 15 ⇒ 5y + 12(5) = 15 ⇒ 5y + 60 = 15 ⇒ 5y = -45 ⇒ y = -9.
From Row 1: x + 4y + 9z = 16 ⇒ x + 4(-9) + 9(5) = 16 ⇒ x - 36 + 45 = 16 ⇒ x + 9 = 16 ⇒ x = 7.
💡 Why this step is performed: Substitute known variable values upward from the bottom row equation to determine each variable uniquely.
Step 5: Verify in original Equation 1
2x + y + z = 2(7) + (-9) + 5 = 14 - 9 + 5 = 10. Exactly matches RHS! Verified.
💡 Why this step is performed: Execute step 5: verify in original equation 1 to advance the step-by-step mathematical solution toward the final result.
✓ G. Final Answer: x = 7, y = -9, z = 5
H. Examination Presentation Strategy
  • State augmented matrix clearly.
  • Show row operations explicitly.
  • Show back-substitution step by step.
  • Verify in at least one original equation.
I. Common Mistakes & How to Avoid Them
⚠️ Common Mistake: Arithmetic error in 5(-22) + 7(15) = -110 + 105 = -5.
✓ How to Avoid: Be very careful with mental arithmetic; write every multiplication out!
⚠️ Common Mistake: Forgetting to apply row operations to the RHS column B.
✓ How to Avoid: Any row operation applies to the ENTIRE row, including the constant term!
J. Similar Practice Question
Practice Problem 57: Solve by Gauss elimination: x + y + z = 6, 2x - y + z = 3, x + 2y - z = 2.
(Answer with working provided in the Consolidated Practice Answer Key at the end of this book).
